# Evaluation evidence and limits

The [ledger](levels.csv) contains 6,814 distinct pack and level identities: 6,020 bundled Classic fan levels and 794 bundled NeoLemmix levels. A row has a numeric difficulty score only when a winning replay supports it. The `completion` and `confidence` columns identify that evidence. The `replay_sha256` column identifies the replay used for each verified win.

A winning replay validates completion in the native engine and supports replay-based scoring. It is not, by itself, a comparison with the source engine. The `physics_parity` column records three partial matches to source replay assignment states and 17 matches to archived Golems replay saved-count and completion headers. The independent `Diving Area` run matches the saved count but differs in completion time. Full parity stays unverified until the rescue result and relevant timing and terrain changes agree against an independent engine or reference state.

## Current result

| Corpus | Levels | Winning replay scores | Estimates without a verified win |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Classic fan packs | 6,020 | 2,309 | 3,711 |
| Bundled NeoLemmix | 794 | 322 | 472 |

The NeoLemmix count includes 160 Redux levels, 117 Introduction Pack levels and 45 Original Lemmings levels. For NeoLemmix, 109 attempted replay analyses failed and 363 levels had no matching replay. A failed replay does not prove the level is impossible. Twelve winning NeoLemmix scores used source-compatible replay input. They are native wins on the bundled level, but source version parity is unverified. The 514 levels in `Original_Lemmings` are [conversions of official games](https://www.neolemmix.com/?page=official); 469 of those conversion scores remain estimates. The non-official backlog is **3,711** Classic fan levels plus three Introduction Pack levels, or **3,714** in total.

## Golems hatch coordinate correction, 2 October 2026

The exact bundled `fan:lldb-393/pieuw01.dat#9` (`Diving Area`) stores its first hatch at X=564. Classic artwork aligns that object to X=544 after its display offset. The Golems source engine reads the stored object X when it places the release point; aligning the release point as artwork had moved it four pixels left. The native engine now preserves the source X separately and releases this hatch at X=573. Original DOS still releases it at X=568. Switching an unstarted level between these rule sets also preserves the correct coordinate. The archive, level and graphics digests match the independently run source player; [the hatch evidence](classic-golems-hatch-coordinate.json) records them and the source engine digest.

The published replay now strictly saves **4/4** on the exact bundled level at native tick **1,373**. Ten replay probes assign difficulty **483.8486705640427**. The selected replay SHA-256 is `91610c2bc4d3bc04245c495f0b8c007844ef281a341660503429fde6d4261c74`; a second strict check passed **1/1** after the replay entered `candidate-solutions.json` and the game routed the level to Golems. The independent source player saves **4/4** at cycle **1,417**. That 44-tick gap remains a source-physics parity defect or an unresolved timing difference; the native win does not clear it. This correction supersedes the earlier Basher-only diagnosis below.

The hatch change affected seven previously selected Golems replay outcomes. Their action lists still won with the same saved counts, but five completion ticks and two final state hashes changed. All **148** selected Golems levels in the affected audit were strictly replayed on their exact bundled archives: **125** base-audit rows and **23** fan-evidence overlay rows, with **zero** failures. The seven changed witnesses received new replay digests and ten-probe profiles. A separate post-merge strict check passed **8/8** for the seven rebased levels and `Diving Area`. [The recheck register](classic-golems-hatch-recheck.json) records before and after results, archive hashes and current replay paths. It keeps the older replay files as dated evidence.

A current-source trace confirms that the selected `Diving Area` replay has the same nine commands as the archived source candidate. Before the hatch fix, lemming 0 remained a Basher at x **608** on tick **130** and x **613** on tick **145**. After the fix, it walks at x **615** and x **630** on those ticks. These positions match the approximate source sprite ranges **613–615** and **628–631**. At tick **195**, the corrected native foot is x **680**, within the source sprite range **678–681**. The eight sampled terrain pixels at x **617–618** are now empty at rows **28, 30, 32 and 34** on tick **130**. The source screenshot also shows empty pixels there. The earlier native mask showed solid pixels. The same commands now win **4/4**. [The reconciliation record](classic-golems-diving-reconciliation.json) pins the command, archive, trace and terrain digests. The hatch-coordinate defect caused the earlier sampled Basher and terrain difference. The **44-tick** completion difference remains, so full physics parity stays unverified. The later 2/4 trace and Basher probes below are historical observations from before the hatch fix.

Portable core and modern engine tests, Classic DOS simulation regressions, and fan library tests passed. The repository learning-path script could not build because SwiftPM's manifest sandbox was denied. A source-built learning tool ran the same export, curation and generation steps, and `learning_report.py report` passed with post export disabled. The Xcode SwiftPM run passed **11/11** Learning Journey tests, including official priority, duplicate spacing and multiplayer exclusion. At this checkpoint the path had **292** lessons: **156** official and **136** fan, with **zero** duplicate objectives, two-player levels or preparation gaps and a largest demand step of **48/1000**. The full corpus report passed. **3,735** bundled non-official levels still lacked a verified winning replay and score; independent full physics parity remained unverified.

## Further wins from unaligned hatches, 2 October 2026

The archived game-mode-3 inputs for previously unscored levels were screened for the hatch-coordinate defect. **30** candidates on **28** distinct levels had at least one unaligned hatch. The corrected engine found **18** strict native winning inputs on **17** distinct levels; ten candidates still rejected a skill assignment and two completed below the rescue target. Every winning input maps directly to archived source replay bytes. All **18** native saved counts match their source headers, and every completion is within five ticks of its source header. These header comparisons are partial evidence only; they do not verify terrain and worker-state physics parity.

Each of the **17** newly scored levels has an exact archive fingerprint, a selected replay SHA-256, a strict native win and a ten-probe difficulty profile. The second winning input for one level remains available with its own score; the lower scored route was selected. Normal pack routing now applies Golems rules only to these exact bundled levels, and a separate post-merge strict check passed **17/17**. [The recovery register](classic-golems-hatch-recovery.json) lists each score, digest, source header and replay path. Its source-byte derivation check passed **146** direct mappings across the full Golems register, with two explicit terminal translations and one older provenance gap. Fan library tests and the full corpus report pass.

The ledger now has **2,305/6,020** verified Classic fan wins. **3,718** bundled non-official levels still lack a verified winning replay and score, including three Introduction Pack levels. The regenerated Golden Learning Path still has **292** distinct lessons: **156** official and **136** fan. It has no duplicate objective, two-player level or preparation gap, and its largest demand step remains **48/1000**. The new wins expand the eligible candidate pool to **1,680**. Full independent source physics parity remains unverified.

## Remaining unaligned-hatch rejections, 3 October 2026

The ten archived Golems inputs that still reject a skill were traced on their exact bundled levels. Three ask for a skill with zero stock in the native level. Three target a lemming that the native engine has already lost. Four meet a native state or terrain assignment guard. [The rejection register](classic-golems-hatch-rejections.json) records the input SHA-256, initial-state hash, first rejected tick, lemming state and remaining skill count for each case.

The pinned Golems assembly calls `AssignMineSkill` directly for replay actions. Its interactive Miner selector checks lemming activity separately. A scratch native build accepted the `Gronklems #1.dat#2` Miner assignment while lemming 0 was falling at tick 59. That lemming fell out before the replay's Floater assignment at tick 235. The other nine inputs also failed at a skill assignment with this scratch rule. This trial produced **no** verified win or score. It did not change the shipped engine. The result separates an assignment-path difference from the later movement mismatch; it does not prove physics parity or that a level is impossible.

An independent headless Firefox run replayed the matching source input and saved **2/2** at cycle **381**, with no source or proxy errors. The source replay, source JavaScript, engine assembly and exact bundled archive digests are in the rejection register. A source frame at cycle 59 remains under ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/source-193-3`. The native scratch route loses lemming 0 before tick 235, so this is a measured source/native outcome difference, not a native win or full state comparison.

A targeted scratch check varied the hatch height by three pixels in either direction and shifted all replay actions across eight nearby timings. None of the **56** altered-start trials won. These variants are diagnostic only; none supplies a winning replay for the exact bundled level. Moving the replay actions one tick later keeps the first worker mining on the upper platform through tick 115. The precise first source/native state difference is still unverified.

The pinned source replay path calls Builder and Miner assignment directly after selecting a worker. The native Golems source replay path now accepts those two assignments without the interactive activity guard; ordinary assignments keep that guard. Replaying all ten rejected inputs on their exact bundles advanced two past their previous first rejection. None won; all ten still rejected a later assignment. A one-tick-later input on `Gronklems #1.dat#2` accepted every source command in a production build but saved **0/2** at tick **397**. Both native workers fell out at x=797. The independent source player saved **2/2** at cycle **381**. The [rejection register](classic-golems-hatch-rejections.json) records the source method digests, input digest, exact initial hash and native results. No winning replay or difficulty score was added. Focused replay tests and Classic simulation regressions passed headlessly. Source terrain and movement still need a separate parity check.

## Directed recovery of a Golems source replay, 3 October 2026

The no-win diagnosis above identified a later missed exit after the first falling Miner assignment was accepted. A directed check varied the two opening Miner ticks across **42** exact-bundle candidates; none won, although two saved **1/2**. Holding the stronger opening and varying one later Miner or Builder tick across another **42** candidates found **nine** native **2/2** wins. Moving worker 0's Builder to ticks **303–311** rescued both workers. The selected central tick **307** wins at native tick **393** on `fan:lldb-193/Gronklems #1.dat#2`. Its replay SHA-256 is `e6faada227d405c39d66b448d3e819f58fb131b59f6f50a87bea3c6ca1e332e9`; ten replay probes assign difficulty **380.351510690784**. The [recovery record](classic-golems-late-timing-recovery.json) links the exact archive, replay, profile and source digests. A second strict replay from the merged candidate index passed **2/2** with the exact bundled pack and the app's Golems routing.

The published source player wins **2/2** at cycle **381**. The selected native replay retains the source command order and targets but retimes the commands; its event offsets from the decoded source input are recorded and checked. The native result is a source-replay-path win, which uses direct Builder and Miner assignment. An ordinary player-input win on this level has not been verified. Matching saved counts with a 12-cycle completion difference are partial outcome evidence only. Full source terrain, movement and timing parity remain unverified.

The ledger now has **2,306/6,020** verified Classic fan levels, leaving **3,717** bundled non-official levels without a verified replay-based score, including three Introduction Pack levels. The regenerated Golden Learning Path still contains **292** lessons, **156** official and **136** fan, with no duplicate objective, duplicate puzzle, two-player level or preparation gap. Its largest demand step remains **48/1000** from **1,681** eligible candidates. The raw-source derivation check, full corpus report, learning-path report and headless fan-library suite passed; the latter recorded **831** passing checks. Two older Golems replay paths in the selected index were rebased to their current hatch-corrected files, and the retimed ordinary replay validator passed **2/2**.

## Direct Basher replay check, 3 October 2026

The pinned Golems replay path calls Basher assignment directly and bypasses its interactive selector. The native Golems source-replay path now does the same. Ordinary player assignments keep their activity, steel and one-way checks. The source-method check and focused replay tests cover this split.

The archived cycle-converted replay for `fan:lldb-165/Giga pack 04.dat#5` previously rejected a Basher at tick **1,205**. The current source-built engine accepts every command but saves **11/20** by tick **6,122**. At tick **1,733**, nine workers remain active near x=890–929 and y=78–79. Both native exits are elsewhere. Two nearby global shifts reject an early Floater on each side.

An independent, muted Firefox source-player run saved **20/20** at cycle **1,733**, with no browser or proxy errors. The source and bundled level DAT files match byte for byte. The source and native `GROUND4O.DAT` and `VGAGR4.DAT` files also match. A separate decoder comparison found the same **337** lemming animation frames and identical bits in all **13** Basher, Miner and explosion mask frames from source `GOLEMS.DAT` and native `MAIN.DAT`. Their decoded section sizes differ only in section 4, which does not supply those gameplay masks. The pinned source Basher method uses a 16-frame cycle, applies masks on frames 2–5, checks four forward pixels at frame 5, and moves on frames 11–15. Those frame windows and probe offsets agree with the native code, though this does not establish terrain-update parity. The [independent result](classic-independent-source-checks.json) records the asset digests and source method digest. The direct Basher rule removes one assignment rejection. The unchanged translated replay still leaves nine native workers active when the source has saved all 20.

## Fixed-rate one-skill solver correction, 3 October 2026

The solver's `--rollout-single` check counted all replay events when deciding whether a candidate had one skill input. An initial `--rate` event made that check false for every one-skill route. The corrected search tracks its own skill assignments, so setup rate and forced prefix events do not consume the one-skill allowance. The rollout signature now includes the parent simulation state to keep distinct rate histories separate.

On the exact bundled `fan:lldb-549/Ji Hoon Earth 1.DAT#1`, a five-second, rate-99 check with fallback 20 and width 32 changed from **zero** to **52** one-skill continuations; neither build won. A positive control on the already-scored `fan:lldb-505/KillerMaster 1 Tame 1.dat#1` used rate 50, fallback 5, width 1 and a five-second bound. The previous source build reported zero continuations and no win. The corrected source build found a **1/1** strict native win after 12 expansions, with one rate event and one Floater assignment. Its replay SHA-256 is `b9ca8ab40c9f47693ff9a200fee04996454471c8cd184eaa382ab8247d4616fa`. This corrects search coverage; it does not add a newly scored level or establish source physics parity. The paired builds, routes and logs are in ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion`.

The corrected search also checked two unverified one-lemming levels with only Builders stocked. On `fan:lldb-15/Mikepak10.dat#9`, it completed **16** one-skill continuations through no-input tick **282**. On `fan:lldb-68/Lemmy556 More levels.dat#14`, it completed **12** through tick **156**. Both exact bundled levels remained at **0/1** saved. These checks cover only one Builder on the rate-99 waiting route; both levels may need several assignments or another route.

Three more unverified levels received the corrected fixed-rate one-skill check with fallback 20: `Bomb Your Way To Freedom` ran **181** rate-99 continuations and **394** rate-1 continuations; `Tightrope Murder` ran **11** rate-99 continuations; and `Lemcon Nine` ran **51** rate-99 continuations. All stayed at **0/1** saved. The no-input routes reached ticks **3,062**, **278** and **1,013** respectively. The exact archive digests, options and results are recorded in ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/rollout-fixed-rate-20261003.json`. These bounded checks leave multi-skill and other-rate routes open.

The learning-path curator now excludes three scored Classic fan witnesses that require source-only replay assignment rules. The report also rejects any future selected fan lesson with such a replay. This keeps the playable candidate pool at **1,680**. The regenerated path still has **292** lessons, **156** official and **136** fan, with no duplicate objective or two-player level and a largest demand step of **48/1000**. Those three levels retain their strict native replay scores in the full ledger for engine evaluation, but do not enter the player learning path until an ordinary player-input win is verified.

A read-only negative test marked one selected fan replay as source-only, then reran curation. Its identity left the path and the playable pool fell to **1,679**. Restoring the input left the saved path at **1,680** candidates. The learning-path report, full corpus report and Python syntax checks passed.

## Solver mechanics routing and one-skill coverage, 3 October 2026

`ClassicSolver` previously loaded every fan pack with Original mechanics unless the caller supplied `--golems-mechanics`. The app selects Golems rules for exact bundled pack or level revisions in `FanLevelLibrary`. On the known winning `fan:lldb-290/JUANJO1N.DAT#9`, the old solver's initial-state hash was `81ca7656dacd85b07d8a50faa6bbe9cc28b916c34265881470fce5662f049bf8`, while the scored app audit hash is `c2ea80fd8f49acb51174e730b22f9feafc99e1d55ee2a89301652fb867bd1ae3`. The solver now uses the library's exact mechanics by default. Fresh source-built initial states match the scored audit on that level, `fan:lldb-433/LDChallenge04.dat#0`, and an ordinary Original-mechanics control in pack 505. Explicit `--golems-mechanics` remains available for diagnostic trials on unselected levels.

With corrected Golems loading, fixed-rate one-skill trials on three unverified pack-290 levels still found no win: level 0 reached no-input tick **106** with **10** continuations, level 3 reached tick **1,651** with **597** continuations before its 20-second limit, and level 5 reached tick **263** with **50** continuations. All saved **0** against their rescue targets. Separately, a complete one-skill sweep at rate 99 checked **1,260** assignments on `Training 05 - Let's Build!` and **380** on `Lemming's chocolate orange`, with no win. The sweeps on `Bashers can bash the wall` and `Lemm The Builder` reached only ticks **86** and **98** before their 20-second limits, so they do not cover the full waiting route. These trials isolate search coverage from the now-correct mechanics selection; no score changed.

A second corrected-mechanics check covered the remaining unverified pack-290 levels 1, 2, 4, 6, 7 and 8. The source-built solver loaded their exact bundled ZIP, SHA-256 `14a4717cc58d0d4e922f5faa75c4a6976fd56c37b1818a9191b04bf025aeecdb`, with Golems clock, release rate 99, one-skill continuation, 20-tick fallback and 40-tick refire. The no-input routes reached ticks **425, 134, 65, 107, 148 and 152** respectively. They evaluated **128, 55, 8, 4, 6 and 24** one-skill continuations and saved **0** against each rescue target. The first three reached their 20-second search bound; the last three stopped when the waiting route could no longer meet the rescue target. Initial-state hashes, partial routes and per-level outcomes are under ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/pack290-correct-mechanics-20261003`. This only checks the stated fixed-rate route and limited continuations. Multi-skill routes and other release rates remain open; no replay, score or source parity result was added.

The same source-built solver then completed a tick-by-tick, one-skill sweep at release rate 99 for pack-290 levels 6, 7 and 8. Their viable no-input routes ended at ticks **107, 148 and 152**. It completed all **694, 1,482 and 1,312** accepted one-skill continuations before its 30-second per-level bound, with no saves or wins. The exact-bundle initial-state hashes, partials and completion flags are under ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/pack290-sweep-20261003`. This rules out one skill on those fixed-rate waiting routes under current native Golems mechanics; it does not rule out other rates, two or more skills, or source-state differences.

## Introduction Pack teleporter contention source check, 3 October 2026

The pinned Community Edition `LemGame.pas` independently checks both a teleporter's triggered state and its receiver's triggered or held state in `FindGadgetID`. `HandleTeleport` holds the receiver active from entry; `UpdateGadgets` advances the teleporter, transfers the worker at its key frame and then advances the receiver until its animation ends. This rules out accepting another worker solely because the teleporter's own animation has ended.

For `Beam Them Over`, the exact bundled pair-4 teleporter has **26** frames and key frame **11**; its receiver has **26** frames and key frame **25**. The current native trace has the first entry at tick **341**, teleporter frame **10** at tick **350**, receiver frame **1** at tick **352**, receiver frame **25** at tick **376**, and a second entry by tick **378**. Those frame stages follow the source's reverse gadget-update order for teleporter ID 10 and receiver ID 12. This checks the busy-window mechanism against source code, but the source run's actual worker states were not captured. The 2022 replay's third-command state divergence remains unresolved.

For `Infinity`, both source style objects have **20** frames and key frame zero. A receiver remains held through its full animation under the source rule. In the native trace, lemming 2 enters the lower teleporter at tick **299**, and lemming 0 crosses its trigger at tick **334**, only **35** ticks later. A one-frame change to the end of a roughly 40-frame paired animation cannot make that crossing free. The source replay's earlier worker positions or the exact source level revision must be checked before changing the shipped contention rule. This source-code comparison adds no winning replay, score or full physics-parity claim.

## Lemmini geometry diagnosis, 2 October 2026

An exact-bundle audit of the **292** Lemmini `.ini` rows found **162** with every exit placement or every entrance placement below Classic's maximum y=163. In **147**, all exit placements are below that bound; in **27**, all entrance placements are below it; **12** meet both conditions. None of these 162 has a verified native win. The source coordinates and row identities are recorded in [the bounds audit](lemmini-classic-bounds.json), generated by `Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/audit_lemmini_bounds.py`. This is a direct placement check, not a rendered-trigger proof for every level or a claim that the remaining 130 fit Classic rules. A source-correct Lemmini engine lane is needed to solve this cluster faithfully; increasing Classic's global playfield height would change official Classic physics and does not establish Lemmini parity. The non-official backlog remains **3,736**.

## Golems Miner one-way correction, 2 October 2026

The pinned Golems assembly identifies game mode 3 as the mode that disables the original DOS right-arrow Miner bug. Its `AdvanceMiningGolem` turns a left-facing Miner on a right-facing arrow; a right-facing Miner turns there only when `HasMiningOneWayBug` is true. The native Classic engine applied that DOS bug in both mechanics modes. The native rule now preserves the DOS behaviour under Original mechanics and permits a right-facing Miner to continue under Golems mechanics. The pinned assembly-byte check and focused three-case mining regression pass. This is a specific source-rule comparison, not full physics parity.

A current-source batch replayed **85** archived, one-tick-translated Golems inputs containing a Miner on their exact bundled levels. It produced **64** completed outcomes, **21** rejected-action results and **53** winning inputs. Two previously unscored levels changed from a rejected later assignment to a strict win: `The Perch` saved **80/80** at tick **1,734** (score **405.449992892272**, replay SHA-256 `c1a5b4474fff628b60bc765c6872d86cd62314180860a8e0e133acc4ae95fa5e`) and `Spiders from Stars` saved **78/70** at tick **4,611** (score **592.4114193179234**, replay SHA-256 `eec1a31a14b655bb223a0c6078f4381a43e894c71cd65e4e51ce8b2c98b4172a`). `About to Break` and `Columns See 'Em` already had strict Golems alternative wins; a fresh exact-bundle replay and ten probes selected them into the main ledger at **465.1974925496231** and **386.1557245834224**. The [evidence file](classic-golems-mining-rule.json) records their archive hashes, native initial-state hashes, replay digests, outcomes and source provenance. It keeps the two earlier alternative records distinct from the two new phase records. The source-action derivation checker passed **126** direct mappings across the nine evidence files. A separate current-source verification passed **59/59** selected Golems replays that contain a Miner, with no failures. Of these, eight levels contain a right-facing arrow; their ten-probe scores and all measured score components match the stored profiles exactly. The repository's headless Classic simulation and fan-library regressions passed. The Golden Learning Path regenerated with **292** lessons, **156** official and **136** fan, from **1,656** eligible candidates; it reports zero duplicate objectives, zero preparation gaps and a largest demand step of **48**. The corpus report now records **2,278** Classic fan scores and **3,745** non-official levels without one. The published `Diving Area` route still saves **2/4** because its Miner approaches the arrow in the blocked direction. These native wins do not establish full source physics parity.

## Rejected Builder and close-loss diagnosis, 2 October 2026

A current-source replay check revisited the **13** unscored game-mode-3 levels whose published inputs first reject a Builder. At the rejection, eight native lemmings were falling, two were building, and one each was climbing, floating or vaporising. The pinned Golems assembly check confirms that source replay assignment bypasses the interactive falling guard. Using that source replay path moved the first rejection in several cases, but **12** replays rejected a later assignment and one completed with only **1/50** saved. Moving only the rejected Builder **1–12 ticks later** gave no win in **156** exact-bundle variants. This rules out one uniform short delay as a repair for this cluster. It does not prove that any level is impossible or identify a shared physics defect. The input list, current-source runner, state traces and results are under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch/rejected-builder-retime`.

`fan:lldb-393/pieuw01.dat#9` (`Diving Area`) is a separate completed-loss case. Its published route saves **2/4** natively. Lemming 2 splats at tick 338, and lemming 0 later falls out at tick 1,191. Moving the Digger from tick 197 to 198 saves lemming 0 and raises the result to **3/4**; lemming 2 still splats. A one-command sweep covered five later source actions within 80 ticks. A directed **8,211**-variant Miner and Digger timing check reached at most **3/4**. The current source-built `ClassicSolver` recovered the known **50/15** one-Basher control (`Helled cavern!`) with `--rollout-single`, then searched `Diving Area` with width 256, a moderate 12-tick fallback and a 60-second bound. It exhausted **23,208** states in seven seconds and saved at most **1/4**. The source-guided 3/4 route is therefore stronger than this solver result. The exact archive fingerprint matches the audit. These bounded tests add no replay digest or difficulty score; source physics parity remains open. Scratch candidates and logs are under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch/pieuw-nearwin`.

The unchanged published `Diving Area` replay was also run in the independent Golems browser player. It saved **4/4** at source cycle **1,417**, with the decoded 52-byte replay matching archived SHA-256 `b0104e7676a965bf5d59582013ec706cc0202a637cb23815284bc43c6e38e8ac`. The source player's `pieuw01.dat`, `GROUND2O.DAT` and `VGAGR2.DAT` bytes match the bundled level and native graphics byte for byte. Replaying the one-tick-translated actions on the exact native level still saves **2/4**; one further Digger tick saves **3/4**. This confirms a source/native route outcome difference on matching level and graphics data. The exact source-state divergence and input-clock alignment are not yet isolated, so it does not justify a physics change or a parity claim. The [independent result](classic-independent-source-checks.json) records digests and both outcomes; headless browser captures and native traces remain under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch/pieuw-nearwin/source-browser`.

`fan:lldb-140/Epic giga02.dat#0` saves **4/6** with its published source input, while the source header reports eight saves. A separate **1,120**-variant check moved each of the 16 skill actions up to 35 ticks in either direction. No accepted single-command variant saved more than four. The attempt is under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch/epic-nearwin`. This result points to a route or physics difference that a small one-command timing correction does not fix.

`fan:lldb-218/PSP Special2.dat#2` gives an earlier assignment failure. Its first Digger request finds lemming 1 falling at foot `(905,26)` on native tick 84. Moving that command 1–12 ticks earlier or 1–10 ticks later still rejects it. At ticks 95–96, the Digger is accepted, but the later tick-522 Basher finds lemming 0 vaporising at `(827,103)`. Moving that Basher by up to 12 ticks in either direction does not repair the route. This is a staged route divergence, so the first accepted timing shift is not a solution or evidence for changing Digger physics. The exact-bundle trace and 50 checked variants are under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch/digger-early`.

The app, fan preview and fan evaluator now select Golems mechanics for that exact archive revision; a replacement archive with the same catalogue number retains original mechanics. The two revised initial-state hashes are in `Artifacts/ClassicProgression/audit.json`. The old fixed-rate no-win sweep for `Out of Line` applied to original mechanics. A fresh current-source Golems-profile pass checked the published no-input route and all **99** fixed release rates on its exact bundled level. Every route saved **0/1**, while the published source header reports **1/1** for the no-input route. The new native hash and bounded result replace the old sweep in the ledger. This is a source outcome mismatch requiring a physics or replay diagnosis; more solver beam width cannot fix the no-input comparison. `Pass Interference` passed the strict current-source exact-archive replay check (**1/1** selected rows) and score generation. The selected ledger now has **2,154/6,020** Classic fan wins, with **3,869** bundled non-official levels still open.

The regenerated learning path remains at **292** lessons: **156** official and **136** fan, eight skill introductions, no duplicate objectives or two-player levels, no preparation gaps and a largest curriculum-demand step of **48/1000**. Curriculum selection now ignores replay inputs after the recorded win; those inputs do not act during play. This preserves the Miner introduction when the curriculum is regenerated. The fan-library tests passed with the complete local Ports resources. A full app build did not reach compilation because its Sparkle dependency fetch could not resolve GitHub in this environment. The source-built native evidence and replay checks remain the available validation for this migration.

## Golems exit contact correction, 2 October 2026

The exact `Out of Line` native trace first touched its exit trigger at tick **1,013**, but the old Golems profile delayed exit entry until tick **1,015**. Its one-minute timer expired before the exit animation saved the lemming. The pinned Golems assembly calls `UpdateGolemEffects` during walking and changes a non-falling golem to Exiting on its first exit-effect cell. `Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_golems_exit_time.py` now pins both method bodies and this branch. Native Golems mechanics now enter the exit on first trigger contact; original mechanics keep their centre-entry rule.

The unchanged, no-input route on the exact bundled `Out of Line` level now saves **1/1** at tick **1,021**, one tick before the published source replay header. Its strict selected replay SHA-256 is `698b46654f2348a4173379f5e0caa9ae76353562076a39fc1a9a061dfcb46ad7` and its ten-probe difficulty score is **37.45**. `Pass Interference` still saves **30/30**, now at tick **1,020**. Its rebased strict replay SHA-256 is `cecb0362c116d3297b1769800b9251b7b3ab028528c6b12e46ecf04dda5bfd33` and its score is **287.83**. Both selected rows passed strict current-source playback on the exact bundled archive. The selected Classic fan ledger is **2,155/6,020**, leaving **3,868** non-official levels without a verified score, including the three Introduction Pack levels.

The changed exit rule kept all **67** phase-corrected Golems alternatives winning. Their stored outcomes, replay digests and ten-probe scores were regenerated from the current source. The headless verifier replayed those 67 routes, five other scored Golems routes and both selected pack-433 routes against their exact bundled archives. All **74** passed. **66** phase routes retained source-byte derivations, and **64** now match source saved counts with completion within two ticks. The focused simulation and replay tests passed. These outcome checks do not establish complete source physics parity. The learning path still has **292** lessons, **156** official and **136** fan, eight skill introductions, no duplicate objectives or two-player levels, and a largest demand step of **48/1000**.

The four failed published inputs from the fourth Golems batch were replayed again after the exit correction, using both retained two- and three-tick advances. `Out of Line` now wins, as recorded above; the other three still reject their first assigned skill. A current-source trace shows `Even Steven` lemming 0 falling at native foot `(746,108)` when its tick-100 Digger is requested, `The single pillar incedent` lemming 0 falling at `(745,32)` at the tick-59 Miner, and `In the lemming factory` lemming 0 building over steel at `(798,44)` at the tick-130 Digger. The one-tick-later variants have the same action or ground conflict. This separates the remaining failures from exit timing: they need source-position and terrain comparison at the first command before a physics change or replay adaptation. The directed trace and replays are retained under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch/mode3-third-retime`; no new selected score follows from these three inputs.

## JUANJO1N Golems-pack migration, 2 October 2026

The archived published replay for `fan:lldb-290/JUANJO1N.DAT#9` declares Golems game mode 3. Its source header reports **80/80** saved at cycle **930**. Moving its actions one native tick earlier wins **80/80** at tick **929** on the exact bundled archive, SHA-256 `14a4717cc58d0d4e922f5faa75c4a6976fd56c37b1818a9191b04bf025aeecdb`. The same events also win with ordinary player assignments and no source-only replay rule. The strict ordinary replay SHA-256 is `43a8b445811e3f91b92f65822f588746fc93da2c5af3f96232cf11bec50fc5bc`; ten timing probes give **276.52** for `Puzzle atractivo`. Source outcome and near timing agree for this route, while intermediate terrain and lemming states remain unverified.

The app now selects Golems mechanics for this exact archive revision. All ten `JUANJO1N.DAT` initial hashes were recomputed; this pack had no prior selected wins to migrate. The source-built analyser found one win and no load or replay failure in the pack. The selected strict replay check passed **1/1**, and the Golems verifier passed all **67** phase alternatives, five other profile witnesses and **3** selected pack witnesses. The fan-library tests passed with the bundled local Ports resources. The full corpus report now has **2,156/6,020** scored Classic fan levels, leaving **3,867** non-official levels without a strict replay-based score, including three Introduction Pack levels. The Golden Learning Path remains **292** lessons, **156** official and **136** fan, with no duplicate objectives or two-player levels and a largest demand step of **48/1000**. The repository learning-journey script could not start `swift build` because `sandbox-exec` denied its package-manifest step; the same journey generator was compiled directly against the current source-built `NxlvKit` and passed. The failed fan-library run used a missing default graphics path; rerunning it with the available local Ports directory passed.

A directed migration screen checked existing selected replays and ordinary versions of the published Golems alternatives in packs 592, 366, 306, 388, 237 and 315. All **7** published alternatives won under Golems rules on their exact bundled levels. Each of the **9** previously selected routes rejected a Builder or Digger after the rule switch, so none of those six packs was migrated or rescored. For pack 592's two levels, the cached published section-0 input also rejected a later Builder at source tick 297; shifting that assignment through ticks 289–305 did not produce a route without a later rejection. The exact input screen and bounded timing outcomes remain under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch/migration-screen` and `pack592-ordinary`. These checks identify replacement-route work for pack migration; they do not revoke the current selected wins or establish source physics parity.

The native engine now offers an opt-in `.golems` mechanics profile with the later hatch rules, a fall counter limit of 66 and the Golems miner one-way rule. The raised fall limit addresses the measured tick-167 assignment rejection on `It's Raining Lemmings`. On the exact bundled `MARTPCK2.DAT`, stored replays passed strict initial-hash and outcome verification: `It's Raining Lemmings` saved **60/60** at native tick **745** with the published input (score **309.46**, replay SHA-256 `bb316f1d40a035afc977e9d3501b3727e6143856814067cc4ece17da8c9b6a2b`). A simpler no-input native replay also saved **60/60** at tick **3,271**; its ten-probe score is **55.04**, digest `4528053ecea16a43614eb58fe09e0fa1f7a1a0e5cdcf4fce3035f443bc1f2b06`, and it is the preferred difficulty route. Under the default mechanics, the same no-input route saved **0/60** by tick **3,218**. `Cliffhanger` saved **30/30** at tick **1,600**, with a ten-probe score of **269.72** and digest `3fd1370c53883f245c26c91104c9bdb7869a6b32b34c6236a0d675c0ba32c42b`. Their [profile evidence and saved replays](classic-golems-mechanics-alternatives.json) are separate from the default fan ledger. The app still selects `.original` for these packs: switching both packs to `.golems` would invalidate several existing selected wins until their replacement routes are verified and rescored. The two source players ended at cycles **743** and **1,603** respectively; the native timing differences and untested terrain changes prevent a full physics-parity claim. The Classic simulation and replay tests passed headlessly with this profile.

A targeted three-second, width-16 solver pass under `.golems` mechanics and clock checked the other four unscored levels in these packs. `Holy Cow!`, `It's a Magical World`, `The Only Way is Up` and `Back in a minute...` each reached a best save count of zero. This was a bounded search, not an impossibility result. Its partial routes are under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-profile-probe`.

The Golems source replay path now has a separate opt-in assignment rule. The pinned source assembly calls its Builder assignment method directly during replay playback, while the interactive selector rejects falling lemmings. Native ordinary input still rejects a falling Builder. With `.golems` mechanics, clock and `sourceRules: "golemsFallingBuilder"`, the published `Pinpoint Accuracy` input passed strict native replay verification on exact bundled `LDChallenge01.dat#2`: **6/10 saved**, above its four-lemming goal, at tick **233**. Ten probes gave score **209.37**; the saved replay SHA-256 is `22042653665c046f8a6487615ad3fa42cb3bfbda9ae5958ee203d0357d3b294f`. The [alternative evidence](classic-golems-replay-assignment-alternative.json) records the archive, replay and profile digests. The independent Golems player saved **4/10** at cycle **362** with the same source input, so this native win exposes a substantial outcome and timing mismatch. It does not establish source physics parity and does not change the selected default-mechanics ledger row. The replay tests cover the distinction between ordinary and source replay assignments.

Before the replay phase correction, the same opt-in profile replayed **16** previously recorded published fan inputs on their exact bundled archives. Only `It's Raining Lemmings` won directly; six completed below their rescue targets and nine rejected a later assignment. `The delivery service` advanced past its earlier Builder rejection and saved **78/80**. A trace showed lemming 1 digging through the floor and lemming 8 falling through the same gap. The level had two unused Builders. A focused 35-tick-placement sweep added one Builder to lemming 1 between ticks 1,200 and 1,540; tick **1,360** produced a strict **80/80** native win at tick **2,194**. Its ten-probe score is **533.04**, replay SHA-256 `32536d2527370629eb075a1f2cafb6282f3392b3fee6c031a6740d53b48a3d8a`. The [saved alternative](classic-golems-delivery-alternative.json) records exact archive and replay digests. The source engine outcome for that modified route has not been checked, and its selected default-mechanics ledger row remains unscored. The bounded checks and loss trace are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/golems-source-rule-batch` and `golems-delivery-search`.

The independent, muted Golems player saved **69/70** on `Scaffolding` at cycle **2,624**. Its source DAT and the bundled `ANTHPCK5.DAT#3` bytes match. The unshifted native input diverged at the first Builder: native movement had already entered a four-pixel fall, so the new ramp began lower and a later worker entered the trap. Moving every source action one native tick earlier starts the ramp from the source foothold. The current production engine then strictly saved **69/70** at tick **2,623**, with ten-probe score **590.11** and replay SHA-256 `f47879a36cdc3d9ac189a53724ded591ca8dec9b7a540fea01092d99b79a53d9`. Source and shifted-native rendered solid-colour masks overlapped by about **95.8%** at cycle 539, compared with **92.3%** unshifted; this approximate mask includes sprites and does not prove complete terrain or physics parity. [The source comparison](classic-golems-scaffolding-source-check.json) records the source/player digests, the strict native witness and the superseded unshifted diagnostics. This result does not change the selected default-mechanics ledger row.

The [published `A Giant Leap for Lemmingkind` input](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/495/1) was run in the independent Golems browser player. The decoded source replay SHA-256 `312b02e8ede4c261d8070b0714ab4d76d6c90e78147213799593eca692ca4584` matches the source record for the selected, exact-input native replay. The source `MARTPCK1.DAT` bytes match the file inside the bundled archive (DAT SHA-256 `f23db5808c2077f72a37733d3d936f875de974b84ade35411356c4047d3a0b43`; bundled archive SHA-256 `02dde42ce00cffb0a126928f96abb6df8d29b45465bb761cc0a88632828ef832`). Both engines saved **48/50**, meeting the **48** rescue target, at cycle/tick **1,698**. The native replay remains a strictly verified win with score **251.70** and digest `efa7199dbb7bc6cc72261774a1e310b5c6f3a6d305d6d9ed10378f76eafc4c75`. This is an independently measured outcome and completion-time match, not full physics parity: intermediate lemming states and terrain changes have not been compared. [The independent check](classic-independent-source-checks.json) records the player and replay digests. The browser wrapper exposed the unchanged source engine after `LoadAsync`, disabled its timer and called `Tick` directly; its saved result and request log are under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/holy-cow-trace-20261002`. No ledger score changed.

The [published `Holy Cow!` input](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/495/9/Holy-Cow) saved **18/20** at **2,676** cycles in the independent Golems player. Its source DAT matches the bundled `MARTPCK1.DAT#8` bytes. The earlier unshifted native replay saved **0/20**, which was a replay phase mismatch rather than evidence of an unsolved physics difference. Moving the published actions one native tick earlier strictly saved **18/20** at native tick **2,677** under the opt-in Golems profile. Its ten-probe score is **406.03** and replay SHA-256 `86e325455cf004c00768e3ff7b54de3e6dee82bf45da0cd2eeeca3761b4408d4`. The matching save count and near timing support source outcome parity for this route; intermediate physics parity remains unverified. [The phase-corrected alternative](classic-golems-phase-shift-alternatives.json) remains separate from the selected ledger.

Before the phase correction, a focused `Holy Cow!` scratch trial also activated all 32 Golems object slots alongside the raised fall limit. With the current hatch rules, the input still saved **0/16** by tick **4,082**; with the later-release hatch rules, its Builder assignment to lemming 2 was rejected at tick **1,306**. Object-slot activation alone did not repair the unshifted replay. No selected ledger score changed.

The [published `It's Raining Lemmings` input](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/496/1/Its-Raining-Lemmings) also won in the independent Golems player: **60/60 saved** at **743 cycles**. Its source archive is byte-identical to bundled `MARTPCK2.DAT#0`, and the native initial-state hash matches. The default fan mechanics reject the unchanged replay's Builder assignment to lemming 1 at tick 167. Dropping rejected assignments in a separate adaptation saved only **2/60**. This measured rejection led to the opt-in Golems profile and its separate winning replay and score above. The digests and results are in [the independent check](classic-independent-source-checks.json). Neither of the two source wins proves full physics parity for any other level.

The source player selects `GOLEMS.DAT` for this pack. [The LLDB engine-variant table](https://lldb.camanis.net/help/uploading-a-level-pack) gives Golems a release X25 hatch offset, A-B-A-B two-hatch order and splat threshold above 63 source pixels. On the unchanged native engine, lemming 1 splats before the tick-167 Builder. An isolated build changed the native fall-counter limit from 60 to 66; that counter starts at 3, so the change is a plausible representation of the source threshold, but it has not been proved for all fall paths. With the unchanged published assignments, this scratch build saved **60/60** at tick **746** under the existing fan mechanics and at tick **745** under the later-release hatch rules. The source ended at cycle **743**, leaving a timing difference. This trial preceded the opt-in engine profile; it did not change the selected ledger row.

The same isolated later-release rule trial replayed all five existing scored witnesses in `MARTPCK2.DAT`: sections 1, 3 and 9 rejected a previously accepted assignment, while sections 2 and 5 accepted the input but saved **0/27** and **0/5** respectively. A pack-wide switch would invalidate those five current scores until replacement winning replays are found and rescored. The scratch binary digest and per-section results are recorded in [the independent check](classic-independent-source-checks.json); the runner and trace are under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/splat63`. The shipped ledger remains at **3,870** unverified non-official levels.

The same scratch profile also replayed all nine scored witnesses in `MARTPCK1.DAT`. Sections 0, 3 and 5 still won; sections 1, 2, 4 and 7 rejected an assignment; sections 6 and 9 completed below their rescue targets. Across both confirmed Golems packs, **3/14** witnesses retained a win, **7/14** rejected an assignment and **4/14** accepted the input but lost. The full scratch results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/splat63/scored-replay-results.log`. No current score or digest was replaced by this rule trial.

For comparison, changing only the scratch fall-counter limit to 66 while retaining the current original hatch rules kept **14/14** existing pack witnesses winning; the `It's Raining Lemmings` source input then saved **60/60** at native tick **746**. The source Golems hatch offset is one pixel different, and the source win ends at cycle **743**. This selective change therefore does not establish a source-faithful Golems profile. Its exact-level results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/splat63/scored-replay-original-hatches.log`. The selected fan mechanics, score and replay digests remain unchanged.

A focused migration search then shifted the first rejected assignment by up to 24 ticks in each of the seven affected current witnesses under the isolated raised-fall, later-release profile. Three retimed inputs won strictly: `The Pool` (49/49, Builder three ticks earlier), `Achtung Lemming` (57/57, Builder one tick earlier) and `The China Syndrome` (46/40, Digger 18 ticks earlier). Their scratch replay SHA-256 values are `72334d78be2be65abc7e103f4a25a9169d2b27e3119cb7dc52ba584491c6c4f0`, `e1f372dce58af35d4f8b1642002723a5847dc6fae3302117eccecf5b957d9752` and `a0af3239c497c770f9ea7e1fb4e45daea2fce9c19f1bc7b39290b30fcda0b306`. The staged ZIP SHA-256 values matched the exact `Content/LevelPacks` files. A separate strict run loaded all three replay files and verified their initial hashes, expected outcomes and wins against the exact `Content/LevelPacks` archives. Four first-rejection timing sweeps found no win. These are replacement candidates for a future source-profile migration, not new shipped-engine wins or scores; the current 14 witnesses remain valid under the current engine. The inputs and bounded results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/splat63/first-rejection-radius24.log`.

A second scratch search shifted each assigned-skill event alone by up to 16 ticks in eight distinct affected witnesses. It found three more strict replacement routes: `MARTPCK1.DAT#6` saves **56/56** after moving its last Bomber 16 ticks earlier; `MARTPCK2.DAT#5` saves **6/5** after moving one Bomber four ticks earlier; `MARTPCK2.DAT#9` saves **1/1** after moving an early Builder one tick earlier. Their replay SHA-256 values are `a87aeebf22c7a936007e41c1a52232aad6094d8a3c1cfa4d4f7c2140dc919783`, `dd5233ea37fb8728c98db6968d4293814e49349a7b98c0fef1aa9a59446bb154` and `eaa2afc1ba7260375a835f880c74f74ae9b57ced06ea81f9a202aad8e990e2cf`. The exact bundled archive recheck now passes **6/6** retimed candidate files. Five existing scored witnesses still have no winning input under this isolated profile within the bounded search. Combined with three unchanged scratch wins, **9/14** existing pack witnesses have a strict scratch-profile route. The source `It's Raining Lemmings` input is a tenth candidate for the two packs, but its scratch completion timing differs from the source. These scratch routes do not replace the selected ledger scores.

Two cached alternate public routes also won under that isolated profile on the exact bundled archives. The alternate for `Stairway to Heaven` saved **60/60** at scratch tick **1,651**, giving a replacement route for a previously failing scored witness. The second [published `Cliffhanger` route](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/496/7) saved **30/30** at scratch tick **1,600**; its source result was 30/30 at cycle 1,603. A separate strict run verified both saved replay files, their initial hashes and expected outcomes (**2/2**). Their replay SHA-256 values are `2ec7107f8f9958e431999b472f8341421a6d69d6cc8f31510bf2358eedc028cc` and `490e17f425def4c93cdf0ac559274315d797a4e12b97f1bafedd64b1409dcccc`. The profile now has winning routes for **10/14** existing scored entries and two further entries with no shipped score, `It's Raining Lemmings` and `Cliffhanger`. A build from the current shipped sources replayed those exact alternate inputs and saved **0/60** and **15/30** respectively. The source and scratch completion times still differ, and the selected ledger remains at **3,870** unverified non-official levels. The alternate inputs, trial results and digests are in [the independent check](classic-independent-source-checks.json).

The [source-family register](source-engine-families.json) now labels all **20** levels in packs 495 and 496 as Golems, based on their LLDB browser-player data. This is source classification, not a claim that the current native Classic playback matches Golems physics. Their existing scored rows still refer to the current shipped engine and its recorded replay digests.

The Classic audit also contains two low-confidence levels from `fan:lldb-595` in local Application Support. That pack is absent from the 535 archives in `Content/LevelPacks` and its `packs.json` manifest. The bundled ledger excludes these two local-only rows; the audit retains them as separate local content. All 6,020 bundled audit rows have an archive SHA-256 matching their current bundled ZIP.

The pinned NeoLemmix Community Edition style set at commit `38d0449f87501798e78ac668a9494848f4aa9649` rendered and ran all 794 levels in the strict corpus diagnostic, with zero unsupported levels or import failures. The smaller style set currently bundled in `Content/NeoLemmix/styles` left 75 levels without the styles needed for that diagnostic.

All 794 bundled `.nxlv` files are byte-identical to the corresponding files in the pinned Community Edition source checkout: 514 Original Lemmings conversions, 160 Redux levels and 120 Introduction Pack levels. This rules out level-file drift between those two copies, including for the five unresolved Introduction Pack levels. It does not establish replay-version or physics parity.

The replay inputs came from the public [Lemmings Redux replay post](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=4374.msg105852#msg105852) and [NeoLemmix Introduction Pack discussion](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=5204.0). The downloaded archives and the full community style set remain in the ignored local build directory; this repository does not redistribute them.

The Redux paired verifier checked all 160 replays against the 160 Redux levels, including after the teleporter and Stacker corrections. All 160 completed natively and again after saved-state recovery. All 160 recorded completion frames matched. Two replays used a different source level version; the native win is valid, but source version parity is unverified.

After the Jumper change, the Introduction Pack paired verifier checked the 113 previously winning replays against its 120 levels after the Jumper force-field change. All 113 completed natively and again after saved-state recovery. The newly winning, exact-version `Counterclockwise` replay also completed and recovered, bringing the pack to 114 verified wins: 106 exact-version replay files and eight with a source-version mismatch. Six levels still lack a verified win.

Before the Jumper force-field correction, a bounded timing probe tested every command in `Counterclockwise` and `Infinity` one and two frames earlier and later. Neither produced a native win on that build. The same probe on `Beam Them Over` exceeded its three-minute per-level limit, so the other five levels unresolved at that checkpoint still needed this check. Six replay files at that checkpoint reported the bundled level version; `Pharaoh's Turnaround` recorded version 0 against bundled version 3. Source-state checks located early differences, including a one-pixel position difference at the second assignment in `Beam Them Over`. A matching version number does not establish byte-identical level content or physics parity.

A width-64 beam search over one- or two-frame changes to all eight `Infinity` commands checked 1,755 bounded candidates on the current engine. The best candidate rescued one of three required lemmings. No candidate won; wider timing changes, level-version drift and physics differences remain open.

A later one-command search over shifts of up to 40 frames also found no `Infinity` win on the unchanged engine. An isolated scratch variant that added four frames to every teleporter wait won `Infinity` with 3 of 3 rescued, but its first previously divergent source assignment still differed by one vertical pixel. That variant retained all 160 Redux replay wins and lost one of the 113 previously verified Introduction Pack wins (`A Different Kind Of Space`, 112 of 113). It was rejected as a global physics change. The shipped `Infinity` row remains an estimate, and no source physics parity was established.

On the current native library, global shifts of one or two frames also failed to win `Lab Workout`, `Curse Of The Gold` and `Beam Up The Equipment!`. Baseline source-state checks diverged before completion. The `Pharaoh's Turnaround` replay targets level version 0 while the bundled file reports version 3; its baseline diverged, and a shifted command addressed a missing lemming. These checks do not establish that the source and bundled levels are equivalent.

The paired verifier also rechecked the 45 winning `Original_Lemmings` conversion replays after the Stacker change. All 45 won on their bundled levels and after saved-state recovery. Forty-three match the bundled level version and their recorded completion frames. The other two have source-version mismatches. These paired native results do not establish full source physics parity.

The February 2021 replay archive (`SHA-256 333d799d9e147e289359a0ba52bfd2e00fa8961a90d2a9f3edc4d7fe5269dd29`) has no replay with the current level ID for 27 of the 38 levels that were unscored at that check. Later archives and simulation fixes supplied more wins. The five Introduction Pack levels open at that check needed compatible solutions or replay and simulation debugging; an ID match alone does not prove source parity.

The older Introduction Pack archive (`SHA-256 9564f1bf2222b3f03a68b3e6fd3856ec753ea3826cee3142a57f923cb247b301`) first supplied wins for `The Guardian`, `With Minimal Efforts`, `The Joy Of Sorting`, `Index Error Out Of Bounds`, `Night Of The Living Lems` and `Just Nuke Them!`. Its 2020 replay files omit a level version. Their unchanged commands won on the exact bundled levels, with ten timing probes and a separate paired check using scratch copies with an explicit version field. Current-version 2022 replay files have since replaced all six scores and digests in the ledger.

The [March 2021 replay attachment](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?action=dlattach;attach=17532) (`SHA-256 9de9d0012b3c7b7660f6e56b8eb1c886a33696d9864f97df337c6001dc004e5c`) supplied three exact-version wins: `Shimmichanga`, `Laboratory Of Explosions` and `The Graveyard`. Each won on its exact bundled level with ten timing probes and won again after saved-state recovery (3 of 3). The replays record no source completion frame, so independent physics parity remains unverified. Four July 2026 replays from the same forum have different level IDs from the bundled pack and were not counted.

The older `Blocker Rocker` replay had a stale recorded spawned count. Source-compatible playback now treats that count as a diagnostic and applies the release-rate input. On the exact bundled level it saved 99 of 99 and repeated the win after saved-state recovery. The paired replay had no recorded completion frame, so this adds a native win and score but not independent physics parity. The same change enabled native replay scores for two Original Lemmings levels.

An earlier hash-ordered Classic solver sample of 58 previously unverified levels produced no further winning replay. A later score-prioritised sample searched 100 levels and proposed 24 wins. The native verifier rescanned 535 packs with those candidates and route templates; it accepted 211 distinct witnesses. Three levels gained their first verified score, and eight existing scores improved. The learning path now contains 507 levels: 317 official and 190 fan levels. Its generator still excludes metadata-only fan scores, duplicate puzzles and two-player levels. A failed bounded search does not prove that a level is impossible.

The three new fan witnesses also passed a strict replay recheck against their current level fingerprints and stored replay digests: 3 of 3 native wins.

A further score-prioritised batch searched 40 fan levels and proposed one win. The verifier now checks fan entries that copy official puzzles for ledger coverage; the learning path still excludes those duplicates. A targeted pass across the 30 packs with known official-copy scenarios increased the local witness collection from 211 to 259, but only one previously unverified level gained a score after merging with committed evidence. That new replay passed a strict fingerprint and digest recheck (1 of 1). The regenerated learning path remains at 507 distinct levels: 317 official and 190 fan.

The next score-prioritised batch searched 50 previously untried fan levels for up to five seconds each. Six solver routes won; the native verifier accepted two first-time scores: `Timpack1.dat#3` (`Slanted Bashers`) and `LEMREMAKE.dat#6` (`Tailor-made for blockers (rm)`). Both passed the strict bundled-pack fingerprint and replay-digest recheck (2 of 2). The other four candidates did not add a first-time score. The 44 bounded searches without a route remain unverified. The regenerated learning path has 509 distinct levels: 317 official and 192 fan, with no duplicate puzzles or two-player levels.

A further score-prioritised batch searched 100 untried fan levels at the same five-second, width-48 limit. Seven solver routes won. Four added first-time scores after replay on the exact bundled packs: `CRISFN14.dat#3`, `CRISFN14.dat#5`, `LDChallenge01.dat#3` and `Lemmings Plus DOS Project - 09 - Medi (Part 3).dat#0`. All four passed the strict archive fingerprint and replay-digest recheck (4 of 4). The other three already had verified scores. The 93 searches without a route remain unverified. The regenerated learning path has 513 distinct levels: 317 official and 196 fan; the duplicate-puzzle and two-player checks pass.

The [2022 Introduction Pack replay collection](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?action=dlattach;attach=19608) (`SHA-256 e437225e184699e71817fd6fe7270b3a4ecb3145884ab4ad4ab1ef61fa710de5`) supplied 20 first-time wins. Each was scored on its exact bundled level with ten timing probes, then completed again from a recovered saved state (20 of 20). Seventeen replay files match the bundled level version; three have a source version mismatch and use their unchanged commands on the bundled level. Their replay digests are in the ledger. No replay contains a source completion frame, so source physics parity remains unverified.

The same 2022 collection also supplied current-version winning replays for ten levels previously scored from version-mismatched files. All ten passed ten timing probes, exact bundled-level replay, and saved-state recovery. Their scores and replay digests now replace the older entries. This reduced the source-compatible winning count from 22 to 12; it does not establish full source physics parity.

Their source versions differ from the bundled levels; neither establishes independent physics parity.

The 16 unmodified source replay files for the then-unscored levels failed the native replay check. This source-code match addresses one timing rule, not complete source physics parity.

The pinned NeoLemmix Community Edition Stacker code places its first brick one row lower when the forward foot pixel is unsupported, and turns the lemming when it cannot place a brick. Eleven unverified fan entries share an initial-state hash with a verified entry. Replaying the available matching routes against those exact entries made 21 native trials and found no further win. The initial-state hash omits exit and other trigger configuration, so a hash match is a candidate lookup, not proof that two levels are equivalent.

A four-input, one-frame search made 1,897 trials from 43 short public replay records across 40 further unverified levels. It found no additional native win. Those levels remain estimates.

The pinned Community Edition Jumper routine checks force fields after each movement microstep. The native simulation now does the same. The exact-version `Counterclockwise` source replay wins on the bundled level, saving 22 of 22 by tick 951, and wins again after saved-state recovery. All 16 recorded assignment states match the native positions and directions, with no rejected assignment. Its ten-probe score is 663.45, tied to replay SHA-256 `2acdf4bf8dd221d1cc9b3287f38eda854e1f58522c6aaf54b274ae16f7099c67`. The other six attempted Introduction Pack source replays still fail natively. The targeted simulation tests pass. These source-code and replay-state checks strengthen the comparison but do not establish full frame-by-frame physics parity.

After this change, the paired verifier reconfirmed 113 of 113 previous Introduction Pack wins, 160 of 160 Redux wins and 45 of 45 Original Lemmings conversion wins, each also after saved-state recovery. A full 794-level reanalysis under simulation revision `8fe32080d8a5b9db37e1302bf68ae25a9ab517de447d2ca27f5110f8157f6787` retained every previous winning score and replay digest and added `Counterclockwise`. It found no lost win. The six currently failed Introduction Pack replay diagnoses remain recorded alongside 106 failed Original conversion replay analyses.

## Checks

The two active native NeoLemmix replay derivations and their ledger digests passed `python3 Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_replay_derivations.py`. The earlier `A Different Kind Of Space` timing adjustment was superseded by its winning unchanged source replay. The 218 Classic replay adjustments passed `python3 Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_classic_replay_derivations.py`. The report generator is `Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/full_corpus_report.py`. The strict corpus check used `Scripts/run-nxlv-corpus-diagnostics.sh` with the bundled levels, the pinned full style set, and `--require-runnable`. NeoLemmix replay scoring used `Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/main.swift` with ten timing probes per winning replay. The paired verifier was built by `Scripts/run-nxrp-paired-corpus-diagnostics.sh`. Its compiled binary ran the separate pack checks because a later wrapper rebuild hit a local Command Line Tools compiler and SDK mismatch. This run used the installed Xcode toolchain for native builds. SwiftPM could not start `Scripts/generate-learning-journey.sh` under the current sandbox, so the existing compiled journey tool generated the path; `learning_report.py report` checked its output.

The 4,459 estimated scores must remain marked as estimates until a replay wins on the exact bundled level. Across the bundled corpus, 3,987 Classic fan levels and 472 NeoLemmix levels, including three Introduction Pack levels, still lack a verified win. The other 469 NeoLemmix estimates are official-game conversions. Some supplied replays diverge under the native engine. Those failures need investigation before a full completion claim is possible.

The [Lemmings Level Database](https://lldb.camanis.net/) publishes watchable community solutions for many Classic levels. Its browser links contain compressed Golems replay data. A 20-page pilot found six initially translatable candidates. One gained a verified native win after the two-tick nuke adjustment recorded above. A published one-Digger replay for the already scored `Just dig!` also won natively, and its source `Fun.DAT` bytes matched the bundled archive. The later 100-page batch and corrected decoder add the evidence recorded above; neither establishes full source physics parity.

The [Classic source outcome comparison](classic-source-outcomes.json) checks 934 selected, unchanged published replay inputs against the published saved count and tick header. Native wins matched the published saved count on 913 levels and differed on 21. Completion ticks matched on 173 and differed on 761. This is independent outcome evidence, but it does not compare every lemming state or terrain change. The saved-count and timing differences are parity investigations; all full physics-parity cells remain unverified.

One published intended solution for `fan:lldb-1` (`geooPk0.dat#0`) was decoded and tested locally. The [pack author identifies geooPk0 as a CustLem2 pack](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=555.0). Its recorded Builder assignment is rejected by the native simulation because the target is climbing at that tick. A scratch build with a higher safe-fall threshold kept the target alive, but the command still diverged; timing probes did not produce a win. This is an unresolved variant or replay-translation mismatch, not a verified physics-parity result.

## Continuing search at 2026-09-29 08:09 UTC

The public LLDB page sweep and the final exact, one-event and whole-replay timing checks have finished. Another bounded five-second, width-48 Classic solver batch completed 500 searches without a new winning route. Its attempted identities are recorded and will not be repeated. The next 500-search batch found one new candidate, `The Sword`, in pack `fan:lldb-427`. It passed the strict bundled-archive fingerprint, native replay and replay-digest check (1 of 1). Its ten-probe score is 263.10. The learning path now has 858 distinct levels: 317 official and 541 fan from 201 fan packs. Its official-priority, duplicate-puzzle and two-player checks pass; its largest curriculum-demand step is 48, while raw replay scores decrease in 415 transitions. A follow-up search of ten earlier partial-threshold solver states had only one still-unverified identity and found no new win. The longer public-replay two-event search has finished and its 15 strict wins are included above. The remaining fan scores need new winning routes or improved source replay translation. No unverified candidate counts as a completed playtest.

A four-event timing search tested 22,048 radius-two variants for the 31 remaining short public replay levels. It found no new native win. The log is `.build/learning-evidence/four-event-radius2/search.log`; those variants will not be repeated.

The pinned Community Edition source resumes a lemming's previous action on the receiver release tick. It also freezes a Builder or Platformer's position when the restored physics frame is at least nine. The native teleporter now retains that action state and applies both rules. ## Corrected public Golems replay decoding at 2026-09-29 10:49 UTC

The earlier LLDB decoder collapsed consecutive release-rate changes onto one tick. A Golems replay step stores a cycle delta, and its cycle advances by the delta **plus one**. The corrected decoder also emits the native Codable release-rate action shape. The byte layout was checked against the public Golems preview 7 assembly and a focused regression test. Its cached-page import verified the source replay SHA-256 and header for 1,851 records; 19 records with a terminal `Abandon` action were kept separate.

The exact-action native sweep produced 976 winning profiles, of which 579 were first wins against the current ledger. All 976 passed a strict exact bundled-archive, initial-state, replay-digest and native-win recheck. A separately labelled terminal `Abandon` to native nuke translation produced 12 winners, three of them first wins beyond the exact-action sweep; all 12 passed the same strict check. These three are native wins, not exact source-action parity evidence. The new replay-based Classic count is 1,850, leaving 4,173 bundled non-official levels without a verified win. A full selected replay recheck then passed **1,850/1,850** bundled Classic fan levels across 316 packs, with zero failures (`.build/learning-evidence/strict-corpus-1850/verify.log`). It checked the exact bundled archive, archive fingerprint, initial state, selected replay digest and native win. This supersedes the 1,268-level corpus check.

The corrected source outcome comparison has 934 direct source-action native wins on 934 levels. Published and native saved counts agree on 913; 21 differ. Completion ticks agree on 173; 761 differ. A separate structural scan rendered and initialised all 3,937 bundled Classic fan levels that lacked a win at the start of the scan. Every level matched its audit initial-state hash; there were no load failures. It found **34 native structural barriers**: 30 levels place every exit in an object slot numbered 16 or higher, three contain no exit object, and one requires 37 rescues from only 20 lemmings. Traditional Lemmix [draws objects in slots 16–31 without making them functional](https://www.neolemmix.com/old/nle_piece_properties.html); the native engine follows that rule. The three levels without exits and the one with an excess rescue requirement cannot win regardless of that rule. The [structural register](classic-structural-limits.json) records the exact bundled source revision, initial-state hash, object slots and rescue requirement for each affected level. Its verifier passes all 34 against the native descriptors and current ledger. These rows remain metadata estimates and do not count as wins. A content decision remains open for the four unconditional structural defects; the other unresolved levels remain under test.

The cached Golems preview 7 `Golems.Core.dll` has SHA-256 `38cb69f18f5055821a496336a948fc30596e56c607c3ddddfb4675be78a5873e`. A direct .NET metadata and IL check found that `Level.ReadFrom` reads 32 gadget records, `GameState` allocates 32 runtime gadgets, `InitializeGadgets` iterates the level gadget collection, `AdvanceGadgets` iterates the runtime gadget array, and `UpdateGolemEffects` indexes that array by gadget index. The pinned [IL verifier](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_golems_object_slots.py) passes. This is source-code evidence that Golems processes the later object slots. It does not establish full frame-by-frame physics parity or a winning route for any of the 30 affected levels. The 30 late-exit cases are now tracked as native Classic compatibility gaps against Golems, not confirmed source-invalid content.

The Classic renderer now has an explicit Golems object option that activates all 32 slots. DOS remains the default. Its trigger list also drives simulation and scene animation. The portable tests passed 80 rendered maps and 120 campaign levels, including a check that a later object becomes an interactive trigger only under the Golems option. The modern engine and campaign progress tests passed. The Classic scene tests passed both of their rendered-state groups. On `fan:lldb-308/TWPAK06.dat#9`, the option changes the native exit count from zero to one; an empty replay still loses. This option is not yet active in the fan app flow or difficulty audit, so the level remains unverified in the ledger.

A source replay exposed a missing DOS direct-drop rule. [A Lemmings physics account](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=4744.0) reports that ordinary fallers cannot enter an exit, while a splatting lemming can. The Classic simulation now accepts the splatter in an exit trigger and still rejects an ordinary midair faller. The focused regression, 120-level Classic campaign soak, and Classic replay tests pass. On exact bundled `fan:lldb-173/hubbart.dat#9`, the published [no-action replay](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/173/10) saves 80 of 80 at native tick 425; its published header says tick 426. The one-tick difference and the limited outcome comparison do not establish full source physics parity.
