Neither is counted as a win or source physics parity.

A third [October 2020 replay archive](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?action=dlattach;attach=15371) supplies alternative replays for all three missing Introduction Pack levels. Their NXRP SHA-256 values are `e8b86b8a1657c84c8ea986cee7b184771909ce480859586f4d7283d659de06e1` (`Pharaoh's Turnaround`), `7a5ff51815c008b27399f85b8c680a6da1755038d9e345f2a8b9891f4d9f6532` (`Beam Them Over`), and `a75dc0921c8a5687aa081370ca894c06b40c48affe0697f84eb89a69bbaa446d` (`Infinity`). Native ten-probe analysis on the exact bundled levels reports `frameBudgetExceeded`, `frameBudgetExceeded`, and `replayDidNotWin`, respectively. The source files omit level versions. A paired scratch check of `Infinity` with bundled version zero saves 0/3 and shows a source-state divergence at assignment five: recorded (156,41), native lemming already removed after falling out. The native trace shows it passed through the teleporter trigger at tick 335 while the paired receiver was still busy with another lemming. The pinned Community Edition source also blocks a teleporter while its receiver is triggered or held active (`LemGame.pas`, `FindGadgetID`); this trace alone does not justify relaxing that rule or prove that 2020 and bundled level content match.

A frame trace of the separate 2022 `Infinity` replay on the exact bundled level confirms the same collision. Lemming 2 enters the lower teleporter at tick 299; lemming 0 crosses its trigger at tick 334 while that receiver is still active, then falls out at tick 339. The resolved current style has 20 teleporter frames and 20 receiver frames. This does not identify whether source style timing, level content, or another earlier state differed when the published replay was recorded. No native win or parity claim follows from this trace.

The remaining published Lemmings Plus I matches received one-command timing probes. Shifts of up to eight ticks across 7,376 variants yielded `Die the Death of the Damned` (source tick 407 to native tick 405; **249.64732026143793**, 20/20 saved) and `No Salvation V` (443 to 449; **365.43454037915785**, 9/9 saved). A separate 6,519-variant pass moved one command by nine to 40 ticks on source routes with at most 12 events, yielding `Two Pathways` (354 to 329; **299.6578630934071**, 66/64 saved) and `Meeting Spot` (137 to 177; **344.71376993540855**, 16/16 saved). All four passed strict exact-bundle, initial-state, digest and native-win checks **4/4** before and after merge. The derivation register now validates all **eight** source replay records against the downloaded archive and selected native replays. Each of the four new levels has fewer than 16 object slots. The distinct 21,533-variant pass over 13-to-24-event source routes at offsets nine to 40 found no further win; its input and result files remain under `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lpi-classic-candidates/retime-radius9-40-long`.

The ledger now has **2,124** verified Classic fan wins and **3,899** bundled non-official levels without one. The Golden Learning Path has **1,600** distinct lessons: 317 official and 1,283 fan from 291 packs, with no duplicate puzzle, two-player level or preparation gap. Its largest curriculum-demand rise remains 48; raw replay scores decrease in 727 transitions. The Learning Journey tests passed 9/9. The source-to-native timing edits prevent a source physics parity claim.

The 39 still-unverified Lemmings Plus I matches received 4,976 paired timing variants within two ticks on source routes with at most 12 events, then 6,696 whole-route shifts within 40 ticks, both with all commands and with only assignments moved. Neither batch produced a native win. The distinct candidate and result files are under `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lpi-classic-candidates/pair-radius2-short` and `global-shift40`. These bounded checks do not prove the routes or levels impossible.

The remaining short Lemmings Plus I routes received 9,465 one-command timing variants at offsets 41 to 100 ticks. The [author's ordered level list](https://www.neolemmix.com/?game=20&page=levels&rank=all) also identified four published routes whose names changed or whose numbered DOS levels differ. Those four ordinal candidates received exact bundled-level native checks. Neither batch produced a win. Their local inputs and results are under `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lpi-classic-candidates/retime-radius41-100-short` and `ordinal-aliases`. A rank number alone does not establish that two versions contain the same puzzle.

The earlier LLDB fetcher retained only the last replay on pages with several published solutions. A local rescreen decoded all 95 distinct archived routes on 47 still-unverified levels that had multiple links. All 95 were tested on exact bundled levels; none won under the shipped fan rules. A diagnostic replay under the alternate Oh No! hatch offset won four of those routes, including `fan:lldb-478/Crazy.DAT#1`, but those are not wins under the shipped fan rule and have no ledger scores. An additional 3,520 global or one-command timing variants for these four routes did not win under the shipped rule. Inputs and outcomes are under `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-alternate-replays`.

The one-pixel hatch-offset diagnostic also replayed the 131 selected Classic fan witnesses with unchanged published input against the Oh No! variant. Of the 131, 120 produced an outcome and 11 rejected an assignment. Only 105 of the 120 completed runs won; the other 15 missed the rescue target. On those 120 completed runs, saved count and completion within five ticks matched the published header in 73 Oh No! outcomes, versus 83 under the shipped rule. The full shipped-rule set has 88 such matches across all 131. The source's [Golems mode description](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=6716.0) identifies other custom-mode physics differences, so this offset-only probe does not establish source parity. A global hatch-offset change would lose 26 selected native wins and is not supported by this evidence.

The [LLDB game-variant reference](https://lldb.camanis.net/help/uploading-a-level-pack) specifies different hatch positions and release orders, a builder shrug/climber bug, splat heights and one-way mining behaviour across Original, Oh No!, Holiday, Customised and Golems variants. Those documented differences are plausible causes of some source-replay divergence, but the one-rule hatch probe above cannot isolate them or justify switching a bundled pack's shipped mechanics. Each published mode and exact-level outcome must be checked before any parity claim.

The 1,870 cached LLDB replay headers cover 293 packs. Each pack has one consistent source mode across its cached replays. This is useful pack-level provenance for further parity work, but a mode number alone is not a frame-by-frame physics comparison. The current native fan corpus still has no independently verified full source-physics parity.

The continuous current-physics solver found a direct route for `fan:lldb-514/ssam1221 Wild 1.dat#2` (`Your time is up!`). It saved **69/67** at tick **1,020** on the exact bundled level. Ten replay probes gave a score of **544.5489169942321** and selected replay SHA-256 `793108dbc7bcc2188267953aa042d7ca0f3378eaeb476e85743c1dbd421ade0b`. Strict archive, initial-state, digest and winning-outcome checks passed **1/1** both before and after the replay entered `candidate-solutions.json`. The ledger now contains **2,125** verified Classic fan wins and **3,898** non-official levels without one. The Golden Learning Path contains **1,601** distinct lessons: 317 official and 1,284 fan from 291 packs. It has no duplicate puzzle, two-player level or preparation gap. Its largest curriculum-demand rise remains 48; raw replay scores decrease in 727 transitions. This native win does not prove independent source physics parity.

Three of the four alternate hatch-offset routes then received 22,546 distinct multi-command patterns that moved each assignment by zero or one tick. The fourth route had 16 commands and was left outside that bounded product. None of the three won under the shipped fan rule. The input manifest and replay results are under `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-alternate-replays/paired-offset-one`. This does not prove that the routes or levels are impossible.

The published [Blizzard of Lemm replay set](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=6294.0), downloaded with SHA-256 `f24faadffbf32954a83b7b94cc44ef432306501a332d7f055c3f7724b86c388c`, supplied 65 NeoLemmix routes. Their titles matched all 65 bundled SuperLemmini levels in `fan:lldb-415`, including two rank-order changes. The converted inputs produced **zero** wins on those exact bundled Classic levels. Inputs and outcomes remain in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/blizzard-of-lemm-replays`; source and bundled physics or level differences need investigation.

The [GeoffLems replay post](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=4247.0) supplied two NeoLemmix replay archives, SHA-256 `6417d8fc016e6cf3bee5b92fa483b7c95e019d6ecdb738227f0ee9348a097095` and `27508328870bd7a963e951e58aad188818d086f0df64de6b8e30e6566492d73e`. Eighteen routes matched nine bundled titles in `fan:lldb-413`. Three of those levels already had verified scores in `fan-evidence.json`; the older Classic audit alone still labels them low confidence. The other six levels received 2,598 one-command timing probes within eight ticks. **None** gained a native win. Inputs and outcomes remain in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/geofflems-candidates`. Evidence counts use the current fan-evidence overlay to avoid recounting existing wins.

A current-physics recheck found 15 unscored Classic entries whose initial-state hashes already had candidate replay inputs. Four won in isolation. One belonged to downloaded-only `fan:lldb-595` and was retained only in ignored research output, outside the pinned bundle. The three bundled wins are `fan:lldb-472/levels/02.LVL#-1` (`The Great Pillar`, **46/45** saved at tick **3,582**, score **480.6931302521008**, replay SHA-256 `e643d2da90ca2c6371e29ae9d9ad00d109fd271a341a61ac2c140f939962d62b`), `fan:lldb-472/levels/06.LVL#-1` (`Hold them back`, **49/49** at tick **3,975**, score **625.3931764705882**, replay SHA-256 `3d856908ccd4619ec17bf8b37730eb602baa6fac462761f8fbcceca2bd25c4f7`), and `fan:lldb-519/Van Clan Wild 2.dat#4` (`The Power Of Three....`, **31/31** at tick **897**, score **468.6484431452167**, replay SHA-256 `143335e0db82136fa811e312c1a893291f96ae548cd8686a38c6fe1ef816b88a`). Exact bundled archive, initial-state, replay-digest and native-win checks passed **3/3** both before and after merge. The full ledger now has **2,128** verified Classic fan wins and **3,895** non-official levels without one. The learning path still has **1,601** distinct lessons, 317 official and 1,284 fan, from 290 packs. It has no duplicate puzzle, two-player level or preparation gap; raw scores still decrease in 727 transitions. These three native wins do not establish independent source physics parity.

The remaining cached public LLDB records supplied 316 published replay inputs for 265 unscored bundled levels in 132 packs. Exact current-physics playback of all inputs found no new native win. A focused one-command timing pass on the 78 routes with at most eight events checked another **6,960** variants within eight ticks; none won. Inputs and outcomes are under ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck`. These bounded failures do not prove the levels impossible.

The separate eight-tick timing pass on 101 LLDB routes with nine to 24 events checked another **24,273** one-command variants across 91 unscored bundled levels. None won. These failures remain bounded replay-translation evidence, not impossibility or source-parity findings.

An independent whole-route timing pass moved every event, or only skill assignments, by one to eight ticks in each direction. It checked **9,982** valid variants of the remaining public LLDB inputs across 264 unscored bundled levels. None won under the shipped fan rules. The inputs and results are under `lldb-current-recheck/global-shift8` in the ignored research directory.

A diagnostic full playback measured all 316 original LLDB inputs on the exact bundled levels. **57** completed without a rejected command but none reached its rescue target; **259** rejected at least one command or otherwise failed playback. Only three completed routes saved any lemmings and missed their target by at most five: `LDChallenge02.dat#1` (2/4), `LeviOtherLevels.dat#1` (2/4) and `Pieuw 2007 Insane 2.DAT#7` (1/3). Paired command shifts within two ticks checked 49, 449 and 17 variants respectively, with no win. The diagnostic results are in ignored `lldb-current-recheck/source-outcomes.log` and the targeted `near-two-event` and `pieuw-two-event` folders. These measures explain why further blind retiming of the same source inputs has low yield; they do not prove a level impossible.

The path generator now accepts fan packs only when their IDs occur in the pinned `Content/LevelPacks/packs.json`; the report independently checks this. This closes the downloaded-pack selection gap exposed by `fan:lldb-595`. The compiled generator, report, nine Learning Journey tests, four exact structural checks, 315 Golems object-rule checks and `git diff --check` passed. The repository journey script could not use SwiftPM's manifest sandbox in this environment, so its existing direct `swiftc` command rebuilt the generator against the package object files.

A scratch generation added the verified downloaded-only `fan:lldb-595` row to the normal fan-evidence input. The generated path was byte-identical to the pinned-bundle path. The scratch input was removed after the check.

`Hold them back` places an object in slot 16, so its new selected witness also received the separate 32-slot Golems object-rule comparison. It still saves 49/49 at tick 3,975 under that rule. The comparison register now contains **315** selected native wins; **11** outcomes differ from their shipped-rule runs, and **11** lose their win. The validator passed all 315 current level fingerprints, replay revisions and recorded outcomes. This single-object-rule check is not full source physics parity.

The published [LLDB route for `Us and them`](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/127/6) saved 50 in its source game, but its unchanged input rejected one native assignment. An adaptive native playback delayed that Builder assignment from tick 598 to 613 and saved 49/50. A separate native trace located the sole loss: lemming 2 drowned at tick 719. The bundled level has three spare Builders, and assigning one to that lemming at tick 670 produced a **50/50** native win at tick **1,287**. The selected replay SHA-256 is `889eac477893cb21d57baafcebb06536e93a7c3cd9570bf45d8b42de844001c4`; ten replay probes gave difficulty **370.8623961500101**. The source input SHA-256 is `92ec5087ccd7931ed30e664505ea167fc075fd0caae4c889a913a282e142f06d`; the derivation and bounded extra-Builder sweep are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive`. The ledger now has **2,129** verified Classic fan wins and **3,894** bundled non-official levels without one. The path has **1,602** distinct lessons: 317 official and 1,285 fan from 291 packs. Its priority, duplicate-puzzle and two-player checks pass, with no preparation gaps and a largest curriculum-demand rise of 48. Raw replay scores still decrease in 727 transitions. This modified native route does not prove source physics parity.

The complete adaptive playback retried rejected skill assignments for up to 40 native ticks on all **316** unscored published LLDB routes. None won directly. Its bounded result is in ignored `lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/delay40.log`; the `Us and them` win needed the separate extra Builder above. For `Rules to fall` (`fan:lldb-269/CRISFN05.dat#7`), adaptive input saved 35/40. One Floater raised that to 36/40, and two raised it to 37/40. A sweep of 1,956 single Digger and Floater modifications found no win. For `Circuit bent` (`fan:lldb-431/LDChallenge02.dat#1`), changing the initial release rate raised 2/4 to 3/4, but a 1,004-candidate rate and single-command timing sweep found no win. These two levels remain unscored.

Eleven still-unscored bundled fan entries share a native initial-state hash with a scored level. Replaying the existing hash-indexed winners on all 11 exact bundled entries produced **zero** new wins. This shows that equal current initial-state hashes do not by themselves prove identical playable puzzles, because the hash is not a complete object-layout fingerprint. The strict ledger still requires each archive revision and its own winning outcome. The bounded recheck is in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/duplicate-hash-recheck`.

A full strict recheck of the current selected Classic fan corpus passed **2,129/2,129** entries across 338 bundled packs, with no failed archive, initial-state, replay-digest or native-win check. The input manifest and verifier log are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/strict-selected-2129`. The 3,891 remaining Classic fan entries are still unverified.

The exact selection and attempt log are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/shorter-decisions-next12`. The final **12** eligible positive partials from that completed current-physics search received the same shorter-decision setting. None won within width 128 and 20 seconds. The selection excludes the newly verified `SeverSet2.dat#3` and the two-player entry. Inputs, partial routes and outcomes are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/shorter-decisions-last12`. Across these three distinct 12-level batches, the shorter-decision mode found one strictly verified win. The other bounded results do not prove impossibility.

A further **20** unscored low-rescue-count levels from separate packs received the same shorter-decision search. None won within width 128 and 20 seconds. The selection excluded earlier attempts with this setting, scored levels, known structural defects and two-player entries; its manifest and results are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/shorter-decisions-low-rescue20`. Several searches explored very few states. Existing exact-level structural descriptors for three sampled cases (`Lemmy is here!`, `blessed_are_the_bricks.ini` and `9) Optical Fibre Land.ini`) each show one native entrance, one functional exit, enough lemmings to meet the rescue target and available skills. This check does not explain the search failure or prove source physics parity. The separate structural-limit verifier still passes all **4** known unconditional defects: three levels without functional exits and one requiring more rescues than lemmings.

A separate **20** distinct DOS-format fan levels with rescue targets of three or four received the same bounded shorter-decision search. None won. The input selection and outcomes are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/shorter-decisions-dos-rescue20`; these negative results do not prove impossibility.

An exact-level native trace investigated the one-state solver result for `fan:lldb-66/Lemmy556 My little levels 2.dat#9` (`Lemmy is here!`). Its initial-state hash is `dece3ab249c5b2f11f069e9f782628781d44cc118c8341cc919a79df452466b1`, matching the bundled audit. The only lemming hatches at `(792, 41)`; rendered terrain has no solid pixel in column 792, and it falls to `(792, 164)` and is lost at tick 94. The level has no Floater, Climber or Bomber stock, and its other stocked skills cannot be assigned while falling in the current native simulation. A release-rate change cannot alter the sole lemming's path. The ledger now records this observed native obstruction as an issue, without assigning a win or a replay-based score. The ignored trace source is `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/instant-loss-trace.swift`. This does not establish how the source game treats the hatch or prove source physics parity. The corpus report still records **2,136** verified Classic fan wins and **3,887** bundled non-official levels without one.

The `The Crossing (Part II)` source route then received **1,600** two-command timing shifts within 20 ticks, alongside 80 previously bounded single-command shifts. Assigning Digger to lemming 1 after tick **322** and Basher to lemming 2 after tick **498**, instead of ticks 320 and 490, produced a **3/3** native win at tick **950** on the exact bundled archive. Ten replay probes assigned difficulty **269.12155590386465**. The selected replay SHA-256 is `54d216b8e501b956f487ee55d4a14c399b0a863fe08b244d5ae24de32e9496ca`; initial-state hash is `8eef3e8f17b9bb1d5998853f5e83f6fee225b6efd7ee07dd749470701e13f598`. Strict archive, initial-state, replay-digest and winning-outcome checks passed **1/1** before and after merge. The candidate sweep and verifier outputs are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/pieuw-crossing/paired-radius20*`. The ledger now has **2,137** verified Classic fan wins and **3,886** bundled non-official levels without one. The Golden Learning Path has **1,608** distinct lessons: 317 official and 1,291 fan from 294 packs, with no duplicate puzzle, two-player level or preparation gap. Its largest curriculum-demand rise remains 48; raw replay scores decrease in 725 transitions. The Learning Journey tests passed **9/9**. Timing changes needed for a native win do not establish independent source physics parity.

The published four-event route for `fan:lldb-554/Lemmings Plus DOS Project - 10 - Danger (Part 1).dat#5` (`Alternative Logic`) saves 44/50 natively. A further **3,872** paired assignment shifts of 3–20 ticks produced no win. A separate native solver route saves **48/50**; **720** single-assignment shifts within 120 ticks had been tested previously, and new paired searches tested **4,800** shifts within 20 ticks and **14,400** shifts where at least one assignment moved 21–40 ticks. None won. A further **1,729** candidates applied the release-rate command before tick zero and shifted two assignments within 12 ticks; none won. A shorter-decision solver continuation from the first two assignments reached 48/50 again. A frame trace of the 48/50 route shows no lemming lost: lemmings 48 and 49 are already exiting at tick 1,019, but the level ends at tick 1,020 before their exit animations complete. The trace and bounded searches are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/next100-v7/solver-pair-retime20`, `solver-pair-retime21-40`, `solver-pre-rate-pair12` and `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lpi-classic-candidates/lpi-danger1-pair3-20`. The level remains without a verified win or replay-based score. This native time-limit observation does not establish source physics parity.

A distinct survivor-first Classic solver batch covered **20** unverified bundled fan levels with positive earlier partial routes. It used release rate 99, width 128, a 20-second limit per level, and 40-tick decision and retry intervals. All 20 attempts completed without a win or an improved saved count. `Go Fetch!` remained at 22/23. The selection, attempt ledger, partial replays and log are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/shortest-decisions-positive20`. The `Alternative Logic` time-limit observation is now an issue in its exact Classic audit row and full difficulty ledger; its score stays low-confidence, with no replay digest. The full corpus report still shows **2,137** verified Classic fan wins and **3,886** bundled non-official levels without one. This bounded solver result does not prove the remaining levels impossible or establish source physics parity.

An attempt-coverage reconciliation first exposed a base-audit trap: many low-confidence base rows already have winning supplemental profiles. After applying the supplemental evidence, all **bundled, playable, non-structural, unverified** Classic fan levels have at least one recorded solver attempt. A further batch selected 20 low-score, unverified levels and used progress-first ranking at fixed release rate 99, with 40-tick decision and retry intervals, width 128 and up to 20 seconds per level. All 20 finished without a win. Its selection, attempts and partial replays are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/low-unverified-adaptive20`. No low-confidence estimate was promoted to a replay score.

Exact native traces exposed two zero-skill cases in that batch. `fan:lldb-303/TWPAK01.dat#7` (`Lemmings Of Mass Destruction`) leaves its lemmings walking in a terrain pocket below the exits and saves 0/8 by its 1,020-tick limit, even with a fixed release rate of 99. `fan:lldb-433/LDChallenge04.dat#1` (`Out of Line`) saves 0/1: its sole survivor is already exiting at tick 1,019, but the level ends at tick 1,020 before the exit animation completes. Each exact bundled level received fixed release-rate inputs 1–99, repeating the older fixed-rate coverage above under the current physics revision. The new search tested every single-Nuke timing from tick 0 through 1,019 on both levels; `Lemmings Of Mass Destruction` received that full timing sweep both at its initial rate and at rate 99. None of these **3,060** Nuke candidates or **198** fixed-rate rechecks won. The third zero-skill level from the older register, `fan:lldb-593/Lemmy556 Superpack.dat#0` (`Ismo Brick`), received all **2,040** single-Nuke timings at its starting rate and rate 99, also without a win. The inputs, native evaluations and traces are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/nuke-no-skill-probe`, `nuke-ismo-brick`, `no-skill-rate-sweep` and `low-unverified-adaptive20`. The first two Classic audit rows and full ledger now record their observed native obstructions. Dynamic release-rate schedules combined with Nuke have not been exhausted, and these observations do not prove source physics parity or impossibility in the source game. The full ledger remains at **2,137** verified Classic fan wins and **3,886** bundled non-official levels without one.

The pinned Golems preview 7 assembly received a separate exit and timer IL check. `GameState.AdvanceExitingGolem` advances its eight-frame exit animation and increments `SavedCount` only when the animation wraps. `GameState.AdvanceCore` calls `AdvanceGolems` before `AdvanceTime`; the latter sets `Done` when the minutes and seconds are already zero. The new [pinned verifier](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_golems_exit_time.py) checks the assembly digest, method-body digests and relevant IL branches. This supports the native rule that touching an exit does not immediately count as saved. It does not prove that the native and source clocks end on the same cycle, explain the two near-timeout failures, or establish full source physics parity. No winning replay or difficulty score follows from this source check.

A further **20** low-score, unverified Classic fan levels received the progress-first search at fixed release rate 99 with width 128, 20 seconds per level, and 40-tick decision and retry intervals. The selection excluded the previous 20 identities and their duplicate titles. None won. `A TOWERING PROBLEM` reached 8/9 saved with a release-rate-only partial; all **99** fixed release rates were then replayed on its exact bundled level, and none won. The selection, attempts and rate candidates are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/low-unverified-adaptive-next20` and `towering-rate-sweep`. These bounded failures leave the ledger at **2,137** verified Classic fan wins and **3,886** bundled non-official levels without one. No unverified route received a replay digest or score.

`The Reflection` in that batch reached 35/40 saved with six Builder assignments. A further **360** one-assignment timing changes within 30 ticks were replayed on its exact bundled level; none won. The candidate manifest and native analysis are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/reflection-retime30`. Its 35/40 partial remains research input, not a verified win or replay-based score.

A separate **3,840** paired timing changes moved two of `The Reflection`'s six Builder assignments by one to eight ticks. None won under native playback. The manifest and result are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/reflection-pair8`; the level remains unscored.

`It's a snowinable world` had a 27/28 native partial with five tightly spaced assignments. Its earlier 1,081 one-command retimes had not won. A distinct **5,760** paired timing search within 12 ticks and **5,120** three-command timing search within four ticks also found no win on the exact bundled level. The candidates and native outcomes are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/snowinable-pair12` and `snowinable-triple4`. A separate full-timeline Basher sweep for `fan:lldb-270/CRISFN06.dat#7` checked **779** ticks outside its previously tested 120-tick radius and found no win. Its candidates are in `crisfn06-full-basher`. `A TOWERING PROBLEM` received a distinct width-256, 60-second-limit progress-first solver attempt at fixed release rate 99 with 20-tick decision and retry intervals. It exhausted its beam after 14,258 expansions in five seconds and still saved at most 8/9; the exact setting and binary digest are in `towering-focused/attempt.json`. These bounded results leave all three levels without verified winning replays, replay digests or replay-based scores.

The solver only raises the release rate when an offered rate exceeds the current rate. Earlier recent batches started at rate 99, so their `--adaptive-rate` flag could not change the rate. Their descriptions above now identify them as fixed-rate searches. A separate genuine adaptive attempt on `A TOWERING PROBLEM` started at its bundled rate of 1, allowed two increases, used width 256 and a 60-second limit, and saved at most 4/9 before exhausting its beam after 16,596 expansions in five seconds. Its exact setting and binary digest are in ignored `towering-true-adaptive/attempt.json`.

A further **20** unverified Classic levels with positive earlier partials and no recorded genuine adaptive attempt received a progress-first search from their bundled starting rate. It allowed two rate increases, used width 128 and a 20-second limit per level, and made decisions at 30-tick intervals. None won. The best retained partial was 55/80 on `Do not break your face #1`; `TOO NARROW!!` remained at 3/4 and `Desperate Times...` at 2/3. The exact selection, solver revision, attempts and partial replays are in ignored `.build/solver-experiments/distance-priority/true-adaptive20`. No replay digest or score was assigned. The ledger remains at **2,137** verified Classic fan wins and **3,886** bundled non-official levels without a verified win.

The published LLDB input for `fan:lldb-374/justdigcomp.dat#1` (`Juanjos Just dig!`) reports 77 saved in its source game; its adaptively translated native replay saved 40/65. Adding Climber assignments for lemmings 7, 11, 15, 19 and 23 one tick after each hatched yielded a **65/65** native win by tick **3,060**. The bundled archive fingerprint is `aafbe56bf545df649edde5ca3bd4e51b8f5c834e52772aaf339003420d473a26`; the initial-state hash is `172a947b95629f69ab15a3a297f9c4c0e62dac6448733cb2bbf3651c2ac8ec84`. The source replay SHA-256 is `1c21fe8e8c7eeae72e2daad4bcae1577d2ea3698beebc4fccb7a5da83ffafe2b`, and the selected native replay SHA-256 is `91e83fcdadbb12e18318a8f8a5289cc1ece4db434c27c78323606b3dd0ba490e`. The Golden Learning Path has **1,609** distinct lessons: 317 official and 1,292 fan from 294 packs, with no duplicate puzzle, two-player level or preparation gap. Its largest curriculum-demand rise is 48; raw replay scores decrease in 726 transitions. The Learning Journey tests passed **9/9**. This edited native route does not establish source physics parity; the unchanged source saved header and translated native outcome differ.

The published `Cliffhanger` replay for `fan:lldb-496/MARTPCK2.DAT#6` saved 15/30 natively. Its 15 right-entrance lemmings stayed active at the time limit. The translated published input for `Umbrellas sold out!` (`fan:lldb-366/LEVIPAK2.DAT#0`) saved 22/41 natively. Its trace showed 19 lemmings splat at the same landing area. A distinct **1,514**-candidate Builder and source-command timing sweep reached 23/41. The same translated replay saved 22/41 under the pinned Golems 32-slot object rule. This is a limited object-rule comparison, not source physics parity. The inputs, native traces and results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/umbrellas-*`.

The 27/28 native partial for `It's a snowinable world` (`fan:lldb-272/CRISFN08.dat#7`) lost only lemming 0. He mined past the exit and fell out at tick 986. Removing the Basher assignment to lemming 2 kept the 27/28 result. A distinct **897**-candidate sweep assigned that Basher to lemming 0 at ticks 90–985, and **141** worker-target variants changed one existing skill assignment. Neither sweep found a win. The inputs, native traces and results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/snowinable-*`. A scratch-build audit found that the first `Cliffhanger`, `Umbrellas sold out!` and `It's a snowinable world` probes above linked an older native library. Its SHA-256 is `0ad496f42f54416076bc6e98d13ba69d2ebdaf627707f41c9137923e95197c84`. They still saved at most 15/30. The **1,514** `Umbrellas sold out!` candidates still saved at most 23/41. The **897** Basher and **141** target changes for `It's a snowinable world` still saved at most 27/28. Current-library logs end in `-current.log` under the same ignored directory. The earlier traces remain diagnostic only, and no failed search establishes source physics parity.

`A TOWERING PROBLEM` (`fan:lldb-325/t3tesla.dat#8`) received **2,413** single-Builder candidates under the current native library. **475** had accepted inputs, and none improved its 8/9 partial. A separate **10,165** two-Builder timing batch placed the second assignment after the first build could finish. **1,848** candidates had accepted inputs, and none improved 8/9. The inputs and logs are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/tower-*`. `Go Fetch!` (`fan:lldb-62/ClamSpam02.dat#5`) received **2,545** one-skill candidates across the first three lemmings from its drowning entrance. **1,690** had accepted inputs, and none improved its 22/23 no-input route. The batch is in `fetch-*` in the same directory.

`TOO NARROW!!` (`fan:lldb-511/KillerMaster 2 Crazy 2.dat#1`) received **477** added Builder or Digger timings on its 3/4 partial. **216** had accepted inputs, and none won. A separate 57-starting-tick probe assigned successive Builders to lemming 0 when the native simulation accepted them. All resulting routes stayed at 3/4. `Desperate Times...` (`fan:lldb-161/Yawg06.dat#1`) received **1,849** added Digger or Builder timings after its Blocker input. Only the unchanged base input was accepted, and it remained at 2/3. These results are in ignored `narrow-*` and `desperate-*`. No new replay digest or score was assigned. The ledger remains at **2,138** verified Classic fan wins and **3,885** bundled non-official levels without one.

`A TOWERING PROBLEM` received a separate exhaustive two-rate input sweep with no skill assignments. The native replay began at each rate from 1 to 98 and rose to 99 at each tick from 1 to 240 (**23,520** valid schedules); the reverse sweep began at 99 and changed to each rate from 1 to 98 at the same ticks (**23,520** valid schedules). All **47,040** exact-level replays saved at most 8/9. The current native library SHA-256 was `d5d925d75d9de337aca9669973a7d655f441ec8bd9dedf42cdc9773a3d50f4a4`. The search source and both logs are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/tower-dynamic-*`. This covers one release-rate change within the stated range; it does not cover skill assignments combined with those schedules or prove source physics parity. A separate recheck showed that an older 1/2 `Simpler complexity` solver partial now saves 0/2 with the current native library, so that partial is not a valid current near-win. No winning replay or replay-based score was added.

`Workers of the World` (`fan:lldb-471/RSRdnt01.dat#7`) still saves 2/3 under the current native library. Its third lemming remains active on the lower route at the 1,020-tick limit. A focused **3,532**-candidate search retargeted one of the two Climbers to him, moved his Builder or Basher assignment, moved the Miner from lemming 0 to him, and checked each original assignment removed on its own. **2,044** candidates had accepted inputs; none improved 2/3. The trace, search source and log are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/inspect-native`, `workers-retarget-src` and `workers-retarget.log`. This search does not cover combinations of retargeted skills or establish source physics parity. The level remains without a verified winning replay or replay-based score.

A separate **14,848**-candidate `Workers of the World` search moved the third lemming's Builder assignment through ticks 116–700 in four-tick steps and his Basher through ticks 500–900 in four-tick steps. **14,084** inputs were accepted; none improved 2/3. Its source and log are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/workers-pair-*`. `It's a snowinable world` also received a full tick 54–700 sweep of its first Miner's assignment (**647** new candidates plus the base route). It still saved at most 27/28. Removing that Miner had saved only 14/28; removing either Digger or the second Miner also reduced saves, while removing its Basher retained 27/28. These checks show why the present route is constrained, but do not prove no other route can win. The inputs and results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/snowinable-full-miner-*` and `snowinable-remove-*.json`. No replay digest or replay-based score was added.

`Go Fetch!` (`fan:lldb-62/ClamSpam02.dat#5`) still saves 22/23 with no input. Its first left-entrance lemming drowns at tick 160, while the right-entrance lemmings reach the exit. After the earlier one-skill search, two distinct **8,119**-candidate paired searches assigned a Basher, Miner or Digger and then a Builder to lemming 0, or assigned the Builder first and one of those terrain skills second. Both searched the first assignment at ticks 54–140 in two-tick steps and the second through tick 220 in two-tick steps. The first order had **1,903** accepted inputs; the second had **4,441**. Neither improved 22/23. The source and separate logs are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/fetch-pair-*`. These bounds do not exclude other skill combinations or establish source physics parity. No winning replay or score was added.

The pinned Golems preview 7 timer check now verifies its constructor and timer IL as well as the exit animation. It initialises a timed level with its level minutes, zero seconds and one signed cycle. `AdvanceTime` subtracts one cycle per call, wraps at −17, and sets `Done` on the call after minutes and seconds have reached zero. For a one-minute level that reaches its time limit, this marks `Done` on the **1,022nd** advance; `AdvanceCore` advances the golems before the timer on that call. The native Classic simulation stops after **1,020** ticks for a one-minute level. This is an independently checked, two-cycle source-clock difference, not full physics parity. The exact pinned assembly, method-body and branch checks are in [verify_golems_exit_time.py](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_golems_exit_time.py). The extra cycles alone do not finish the exit animations that began at native tick 1,019 in `Out of Line` and the earlier 48/50 `Alternative Logic` route. The native timer has not yet been changed because a clock change affects the exact initial-state hashes and requires revalidation of existing fan replays.

An isolated native simulation with the source clock's two extra ticks found a **50/50** `Alternative Logic` candidate on the exact bundled level. The same four-command replay passes the current native initial-state-hash check but saves only **49/50** at tick 1,020. With only its configured time limit extended to 1,022 ticks, the final lemming completes its exit animation at tick 1,022 and the route saves **50/50**. The candidate is retained separately at [source-clock-candidates/alternative-logic.json](source-clock-candidates/alternative-logic.json), SHA-256 `4539c7844b0951a65b49d2fd08094c21114a0c064d191173c11e5588c2a02ddbf`, initial-state hash `58d8da50ab5991d1f70f24045422ad9540ef9aca7b7e4d1cbb3d5bc02de3708a`, and audit source revision `bc6910d89e3d4625677d173dd9289ac64e19378a58c5ae9c2f2285875fdfeede`. The source-clock experiment is in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/lldb-current-recheck/adaptive/timeout-*`. The existing `Alternative Logic` ledger row remains unverified under shipped fan playback. The same two-tick extension left `Out of Line` at 0/1. The earlier **4,800** and **14,400** paired-retime candidates also stayed at most 49/50 under the source-clock trial; the winning input came from a distinct earlier 3–20-tick paired search.

The full Classic fan clock-migration audit replayed all **2,138** previously verified fan witnesses on their exact bundled levels under the current native clock and under an otherwise identical simulation with two additional time-limit ticks. All **2,138** retained a strict win. **2,137** initial-state hashes changed; the one unchanged level has no timed clock. Completion ticks changed on 67 levels, and saved counts changed on three: two levels with a zero rescue requirement gained one or two saved lemmings, while `ssam1221 Wild 1.dat#2` rose from 69 to 75 saved. No winning witness was lost. The [per-level audit](classic-fan-clock-audit.json) has SHA-256 `a6b5c4811ba5c3859c46d2827d893591fa9d2f78d87d63423ddc3c35a06dfe2d`; [ClassicFanClockAudit](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/ClassicFanClockAudit/main.swift) holds the reproducible check. It does not itself migrate the stored replay hashes, recompute 10-probe scores or establish full source physics parity. `ClassicDOSSimulation` now accepts an explicit `.golems` clock for a staged migration, while its default remains the current DOS clock.

The migration audit also exposed a candidate-solution index collision for the official duplicate `fan:lldb-475/Taxing.DAT#20` and three later copies: the common initial-state-hash key pointed to a different replay. The ledger's SHA-256 `1bd3c79efc389226671828b915b37667279fa7ab0f7f3249d5c7a4cb604cde58` matches the existing `Taxing 21` witness in `Resources/Hints/solutions.json`. That witness strictly won **20/20** at tick **1,755** on the exact `fan:lldb-475` archive under both clock settings. Its current-clock 10-probe score remained **346.03731123790834**; the isolated source-clock score is **346.0132128914199**. The digest-keyed witness has been restored to `candidate-solutions.json`, so the shared hash no longer selects the other copy for this ledger row. The object-slot comparison verifier and learning report pass after this correction.

The explicit `.golems` clock API then replayed `Alternative Logic` strictly on its exact bundled archive. The [rebased replay](source-clock-candidates/alternative-logic-replay.json) saved **50/50** at tick **1,022**, with initial-state hash `e1bb3e7abcadb0cdf26305371e18932e9b6b30309b4a5e1b11a0a1190faef98c` and replay SHA-256 `f8f39131e3ae2b5f5dab781223bbd68f49d11e15a196c620c8554be3120fd5ad`. Ten replay probes assigned [difficulty **339.69666666666666**](source-clock-candidates/alternative-logic-profile.json). [AlternativeLogicSourceClock](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/AlternativeLogicSourceClock/main.swift) checks the archive revision, strict win, outcome and score. This is a verified native win under the opt-in source clock. It is not yet a win under shipped fan playback, so the main ledger count and learning path remain unchanged until the clock and all affected evidence migrate together.

The staged source-clock rescore now has a tool at [ClassicFanClockRescore](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/ClassicFanClockRescore/main.swift). It checks the old replay digest and exact archive revision, rebases each witness to the audited source-clock hash, verifies the native win, and runs ten difficulty probes. The first **100/100** routes passed with no errors. Their staged records also match the full clock audit by identity and initial-state hash. `fan:lldb-15/Mikepak10.dat#1` exposed a 10,202-tick win beyond the analyser's former 10,200-tick default. `ClassicDifficultyAnalysis` now uses the configured time limit and expected completion tick for its replay and probe limit. A focused test and the Classic DOS regression suite pass. The remaining rescore runs in four non-overlapping ranges: 0–600, 600–1200, 1200–1700 and 1700–2138. The corresponding ignored checkpoint files are `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/rescored-v2.json`, `rescored-v2-600-1200.json`, `rescored-v2-1200-1700.json` and `rescored-v2-1700-2138.json`. Each writes after 25 routes and resumes from its own records. These staged scores do not yet change the ledger.

`ClassicCorpus` now accepts `CLASSIC_FAN_CLOCK=golems` and `CLASSIC_AUDIT_FAN_ONLY=1` for a staged hash audit. The fan-only mode limits itself to the bundled `LevelPacks` folder and leaves official evidence untouched. It completed all **6,020** bundled Classic fan levels with **zero** load failures in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/baseline/audit.json`. All identities and archive revisions match the published audit. **6,019** initial-state hashes changed; all **2,138** previously verified entries match the separate full clock audit's new hashes. The ledger, shipped fan playback and learning path still use the current DOS clock.

[stage_classic_fan_clock_migration.py](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/stage_classic_fan_clock_migration.py) can combine the four complete rescore files with that bundled hash audit. It refuses partial input and writes only to a caller-supplied staging folder. The resulting evidence still needs dependent source-comparison records, ledger generation, learning-path checks and shipped fan playback validation before promotion.

The public [LLDB latest-solutions index](https://lldb.camanis.net/) supplied 21 new solution pages for unverified bundled Classic fan levels. Seven of their eight current pack ZIPs are byte-identical to the bundled ZIPs. The current `ANTHPCK4` ZIP adds `Snow Lev 6` before four bundled Snow levels, shifting their public page numbers by one. The audit matched public pages 7–10 to bundled `ANTHPCK4.DAT#6`–`#9` by title; public `Snow Lev 6` is outside the bundled ledger. An initial ad hoc importer used an obsolete Golems cycle rule, so its 42 native checks and subsequent timing sweeps were discarded. The same cached source bytes were re-decoded with [decode_golems_replays.py](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/decode_golems_replays.py), which advances each replay step by its encoded cycle delta plus one and applies rate changes on every step. One of the 21 first fragments ends in a source `Abandon` command and is kept separate. The remaining 20 first fragments and one alternate fragment passed source-title checks before [ClassicFanCandidateAudit](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/ClassicFanCandidateAudit/main.swift) checked exact bundled archive revisions, initial hashes and native outcomes under both clock settings.

Three corrected first-fragment inputs won under both native clocks on the exact bundled `fan:lldb-224` archive. `Snow Lev 7` (`ANTHPCK4.DAT#6`) saved **40/20** at tick **5,832**, scored **410.85796431826657** and has selected DOS replay SHA-256 `1a422df6a9e808848f9e4610364e9d64c24f8fd3c9df70e724c3dad96d4ad713`. `Snow Lev 9` (`#8`) saved **75/75** at tick **970**, scored **357.10393180918174** and has SHA-256 `8dd12d6931dff68c68391d6066070771ea295b43bd15dfc96b310c135cde0147`. `Snow Lev 10` (`#9`) saved **80/72** at tick **4,032**, scored **394.30320856492904** and has SHA-256 `fdf3c652ed88b872acfd0d5448cfd7e3d4f0aee12b9871064bfe8de30e2dc273`. Each profile used ten replay probes. The selected pack recheck passed **8/8** known wins after merge, including these three. The source replay digests are recorded with their separate source-clock profiles and replays in [canonical-lldb-224.json](source-clock-candidates/canonical-lldb-224.json). These are native wins and scores; they do not establish source physics parity. The corrected 42 native checks contain six wins across those three levels, eight non-winning completed runs and 28 rejected assignments. The checked source bytes and outcomes remain in ignored `.build/learning-evidence/lldb-latest-canonical-0`, `lldb-latest-canonical-1` and `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/source-clock-new-search`.

The regenerated ledger has **2,141** verified Classic fan wins and **3,882** bundled non-official levels without one. The Golden Learning Path has **1,612** distinct lessons: 317 official and 1,295 fan. Its report and all **9/9** Learning Journey tests pass, including official priority, duplicate-puzzle and two-player exclusion. The selected source-outcome register has 132 unchanged-input comparisons; 116 agree on saved count. Three new Snow-level comparisons entered, while two earlier records left because their published inputs no longer match the selected native witness. None is full independent physics parity. The three new opt-in source-clock witnesses are kept separately while the existing 2,138-witness clock rescore continues; the migration stage now requires both sets.

Seven further bounded source-clock solver attempts targeted previously unverified `PRACTICE: BUILDER`, `VANDALISM !!`, `Lemcon Nine`, `Star Unit 17`, `Stop, Block and Blow`, `Under and out` and `Livin' A Lie`. These bounds do not establish impossibility.

The downloaded ZIPs for packs 386, 437, 508 and 509 match their bundled ZIPs byte for byte; the digests are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/source-clock-new-search/current-structural-pack-comparison.json`. `Gronklems -1.dat#1` requires 37 rescues from 20 lemmings; `LDChallenge08.dat#1`, `KillerMaster 1 Wicked 1.dat#3` and `KillerMaster 1 Havoc 2.dat#7` encode no exit object. `Lemmings fall first` in bundled `fan:lldb-232/JANNPCK2.DAT#9` has two published solution fragments. Its first ends in a source `Abandon` command; mapping that command to native nuke still rejects the first Floater at tick 88. The second has source replay SHA-256 `e32c97716e67762cad357fd0f4907884ad4ae3a6428d3b1a55ed5b13d1f74963` and a published 43-saved header; its corrected input also rejects Floater on lemming 0 at tick 88 under both native clocks. The earlier 38- and 41-candidate timing sweeps used the obsolete decoder and cannot diagnose this corrected input. No native win, score or parity claim follows for this level.

A further cached-page pass used the canonical Golems decoder on 59 first solution fragments and 49 distinct alternate fragments for still-unverified bundled Classic fan levels. All 108 candidates passed public-title and exact bundled archive checks before strict native playback under both clock settings. The resulting **216** checks produced **72** completed losses, **144** rejected command sequences and **zero** wins. The corrected published input for `fan:lldb-489/Genesis Tricky.dat#29` (`Don't follow me.`) reports 10 saved in the source game but saves 9/10 on the exact bundled native level under both clocks. Its four events contain one release-rate change and three Miner assignments. A focused native search moved each Miner alone by up to 30 ticks (**360** candidates) and each pair by up to eight ticks (**1,536** candidates) across both clocks. The best remained 9/10; **870** candidates completed without command rejection. The input source replay SHA-256 is `6a38ddd07f6d9fe1c0293c353fd88cc94888d0b1bdf3a118105aebcdb3c815ab`; the source and bounded search are in ignored `.build/learning-evidence/lldb-cached-canonical-2026-09-30` and `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/source-clock-new-search/genesis-retime`. The level remains without a verified native win or score, and the source-versus-native saved-count difference remains a parity question.

The current public `Genesis Tricky` pack ZIP is byte-identical to the bundled archive: both SHA-256 values are `d3a60e754dd580f50f3c846b76a977903ccb3ca4997613cd7cde7879d632b080`. The comparison is recorded in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/source-clock-new-search/genesis-current-pack-comparison.json`. This removes current pack-byte drift as an explanation for the 10-versus-9 saved-count difference, but a published replay header is still only an outcome reference; the precise physics divergence is not yet isolated.

An exact native trace of that input shows lemming 0 splatting at `(932,128)` on tick 551; the remaining nine leave through the exit by tick 710 under both clocks. Further bounded probes moved the release-rate command across a 200-tick window or changed its value to each lower rate (**596** candidates), placed the last Miner at every tick from 100 to 600 (**1,002** candidates), and reassigned one of the three Miner commands to each other lemming (**54** candidates). None saved the lost lemming. The attempt register in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/source-clock-new-search/genesis-retime/attempt.json` covers **3,548** candidate routes across these and the earlier timing searches; **1,852** completed without command rejection and none won. These failures narrow this source route but do not prove the level unsolvable or identify the source physics difference.

The first Miner was also tested at every tick from 80 to 260 and the second at every tick from 200 to 450, with the other source commands unchanged. These **864** further checks produced **362** completed routes, no win and a best result of 9/10. The recorded `Don't follow me.` search now totals **4,412** candidates, of which **2,214** completed without command rejection. This remains a bounded input search, not a parity proof or impossibility result.

The current [Introduction Pack forum thread](https://www.lemmingsforums.net/index.php?topic=5204.0) was checked again for the three unresolved bundled levels. Its July 2026 replay attachments cover three newly added Objects & Functions levels outside the bundled 120-level set; the thread has no newer attached solution for `Pharaoh's Turnaround`, `Beam Them Over` or `Infinity`. Their previously tested source inputs remain the available candidates. This is a source-availability observation, not a native win or physics result.

Five more unverified bundled Classic fan levels with current solver partials at 1/2, 1/2, 2/3, 3/4 and 8/9 saved received a focused replay search. Each exact archive and initial hash was checked before probing every release rate, each existing skill assignment up to 80 ticks earlier or later, and each assignment on up to ten candidate lemmings under both native clocks. The **4,216** candidates produced **2,330** completed runs and no win; the best saved counts stayed at the earlier solver partials. The selected inputs, per-level results, binary digest and retained first-win policy are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/source-clock-new-search/near-wins-five`. These checks do not prove the levels impossible, establish source parity or justify replay-based scores.

A separate Golems-clock solver batch covered ten other bundled Classic fan levels with positive prior partial routes. It used a 20-second, width-128 progress-first search with adaptive release rate and shorter decisions. All ten exact archive and staged initial-hash checks passed; ten partial routes were retained, but none won. The attempts and selection are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/golems-nearwin10`. No score or replay digest was added.

The pinned Golems assembly's `AdvanceFallingGolem` method branches past splatting when fall height is at most 60. The native Classic rule also uses 60. [verify_golems_exit_time.py](../../Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_golems_exit_time.py) now checks this exact source method body and native constant alongside the earlier exit and timer checks. It passes. This is one independently checked rule; it does not explain the `Don't follow me.` replay's lost lemming or establish full physics parity.

## Current ledger and learning path, 30 September 2026

The Golems-clock migration is now in the published evidence. The strict verifier replayed all **2,142** measured bundled Classic fan wins on their exact bundled levels. It covered 341 disjoint packs. The first 271 packs had one verifier-only failure: `Mikepak10.dat#1` wins at tick 10,202, beyond the verifier's former 10,200-tick default. A limit based on the level time and recorded win time verified that pack again at **3/3**. The remaining checks passed at **333/333**, **398/398** through a saved checkpoint, **131/131** and **38/38**, with no other failures. The app's solution viewer now uses the same level-aware replay limit. This check proves native replay completion; it does not prove source physics parity.

The [full ledger](levels.csv) contains **6,020** bundled Classic fan levels and **794** bundled NeoLemmix levels. It records **2,144** verified Classic fan wins and **322** verified NeoLemmix wins. The `score` and `replay_sha256` cells are blank for every row without a verified win. **3,879** bundled non-official levels still lack a verified native win and replay-based score: **3,876** Classic fan levels and three Introduction Pack levels. `Alternative Logic` (`fan:lldb-554`, Danger Part 1, level 6) is the new Classic fan win in the clock migration: 50/50 saved, ten timing probes, score **339.70**, replay SHA-256 `f8f39131e3ae2b5f5dab781223bbd68f49d11e15a196c620c8554be3120fd5ad`. Its source physics parity is not checked.

A separate ten-level Golems-clock search tested unverified fan levels with earlier partial routes. It used a 20-second limit, width 128, progress ranking, adaptive release rate and shorter decision intervals. Eight bounded searches did not win. Two candidates won on their exact bundled levels, received ten-probe scores and passed a separate strict **2/2** replay check: `fan:lldb-131/joe04.dat#8` (`Eater of Luck`) saved 60/60, scored **252.62**, replay SHA-256 `065df9c83c597cee22aa484189ec3a2803e370c195ce974113cadead62f9109d`; `fan:lldb-298/LEMERINewlvls.dat#7` (`LemEdit generated Level`) saved 20/20, scored **564.51**, replay SHA-256 `77ba3ea853c0196174bf0158ac336e738b9e8adb15e5797d7f616caa640f5cb8`. The attempt register and exact replay checks are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/new-golems-nearwin10`. Neither win proves source physics parity.

The published Golems object-slot report still compares **315** current native witnesses. It finds 11 changed outcomes, including 11 lost wins and seven replay errors under the alternate object rules. One separately verified alternate Golems-object route wins. The source-outcome report compares **134** published inputs and finds 16 saved-count differences. The pinned exit, timer and safe-fall source checks pass, but **zero** levels have full independent frame-by-frame physics parity. The four exact bundled Classic levels with structural limits remain unscored: three have no functional exit and one requires 37 saves from 20 lemmings. Their evidence is in `classic-structural-limits.json`; a source-faithful winning replay cannot be supplied for these exact inputs under the current rules.

The [learning path](../LearningJourney/summary.json) has **292** distinct lessons from **1,542** eligible candidates: **155** official and **137** fan levels. Its checks find no duplicate teaching objective, repeated initial-state hash, repeated fan scenario, official puzzle copy, two-player level or preparation gap. The largest curriculum demand step is 49/1000. All 1,295 previously shipped fan hint hashes were mapped to the current Golems-clock state hashes. One newly selected fan identity shares an identical starting state and replay with an existing hinted level, so the shipped map still has 1,295 keys. The 218 published Classic source-replay derivations remain historical DOS-clock inputs and pass against the pinned old/new clock audit; the eight NeoLemmix-to-Classic derivations were updated to the current Golems-clock replay digests and scores and pass with their source bytes.

Validation passed: the 11 `LearningJourneyTests`, strict fan replay checks, the 315-witness object report, four structural-limit checks, 218 historical Classic derivations, eight NeoLemmix-to-Classic derivations, two general replay derivations, the pinned Golems source-rule check, app-source typechecking, Python compilation and `git diff --check`. SwiftPM tests needed `--disable-sandbox` in this environment. The learning path was generated with the repository's three build steps using a direct Swift compile because the repository script invokes SwiftPM without that flag. The full app package is still unverified: an earlier build compiled the macOS executable but failed while packaging `.build/AppIcon.iconset` as an invalid iconset.

Two further disjoint Golems-clock batches tested **20** still-unverified bundled Classic fan levels with positive earlier partial routes. Each batch used a 20-second, width-128 progress-first search with adaptive release rate and 40-tick fallback and refire intervals. All **20** exact archive and initial-state checks passed; none produced a winning replay. The selection manifests, retained partials and complete attempts are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/new-golems-nearwin10-b` and `new-golems-nearwin10-c`. The second batch includes `The Turnaround!`, whose 99/100 earlier partial came from a different solver revision and clock; its current Golems-clock search saved at most 19/100. A separate 91-trial Golems-clock retiming of the historical `Someone just had to spoil it all` partial produced no distinct winning witness. Its exact-level input, source route and results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/garjen-old-partial-golems`. These bounded failures add no replay digest, difficulty score or source physics parity claim. The ledger remains at **2,144** verified Classic fan wins and **3,879** bundled non-official levels without one.

Every candidate matched its exact bundled archive revision and recorded initial-state hash. All **20** searches finished without a win. Its selection, retained partials and attempts are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/new-golems-nearwin20-d`.

Eleven still-unverified fan entries share an initial-state hash with a verified witness. Each witness was transplanted to the exact bundled target and replayed under the Golems clock; none won. The target/source pairs, witness files and native results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/identical-state-clones`. All eleven target/source pairs have **different fan scenario hashes**. The replay initial-state hash covers terrain and current counters but omits the placed object configuration, so a hash match alone does not establish an identical puzzle or a transferable solution. No target received a digest or score from this check. The Golden Learning Path still excludes repeated initial hashes; this conservative exclusion can also omit distinct object-layout puzzles. The ledger remains at **2,144** verified Classic fan wins and **3,879** bundled non-official levels without one.

The published `Don't follow me.` route (`fan:lldb-489/Genesis Tricky.dat#29`) still saves 9/10 on the exact bundled level: the first lemming splats after his third Miner assignment, while the other nine exit. The level has three Miners and no other skills. A new **3,537**-candidate Golems-clock search reassigned one of the three Miners to lemmings 1–9 with timing offsets from 50 ticks earlier to 80 ticks later; **2,607** inputs were accepted, and none won. A separate 60-second, width-256 solver continuation forced the published rate change and first two Miner assignments but found no win. The source, candidates and results are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/source-clock-new-search/genesis-retarget*` and `genesis-forced-prefix`. The pinned source safe-fall threshold is also 60, so this observed loss does not by itself establish a threshold mismatch. The source-versus-native outcome difference remains unresolved; no score or full physics parity claim follows.

A distinct low-estimate Golems-clock batch searched **20** previously untested bundled Classic fan levels, excluding the two known zero-skill timing cases. It used the same 20-second, width-128 progress-first setting, with one scenario per selection. Every exact bundled archive revision and initial-state hash matched its attempt; no solver route won. The selection, partials and attempts are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/new-golems-low20-e`. The metadata estimates for those rows remain unverified and their ledger scores remain blank.

A focused exact-level playtest then solved `fan:lldb-12/Mikepak07.dat#3` (`PRACTICE: BUILDER`). A single-Builder sweep was stopped without a win; a no-input trace then showed the exit at x=1016, y=64 above the ground route at about y=142, where one stairway cannot reach it. Ten replay probes assigned difficulty **71.28416699333593**. The controller, witness, ten-probe profile and strict result are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/practice-builder`. The [full ledger](levels.csv) now has **2,145** verified Classic fan wins and **322** verified NeoLemmix wins. **3,878** bundled non-official levels still lack a strict winning replay and replay-based score: **3,875** Classic fan levels and the same three Introduction Pack levels. The Golden Learning Path was regenerated from **1,543** eligible candidates. It retains **292** lessons, now **156** official and **136** fan, with no duplicate objective, initial-state hash, fan scenario, official puzzle copy, two-player level or preparation gap; its largest curriculum-demand step is **48/1000**. The curriculum now permits a low-demand, non-beginner verified witness to introduce a skill in Intermediate when no beginner-ranked witness qualifies, and it includes a second Climber practice before the first three-skill scouting route. This restored eight basic skill introductions and zero preparation gaps after the new score changed candidate selection. The 11 `LearningJourneyTests`, 315-witness object report, four structural-limit checks, 218 historical Classic derivations, eight NeoLemmix-to-Classic derivations, two general replay derivations, pinned Golems source-rule check, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. ## Practice Steel replay, 1 October 2026

## JEFFPCK6 exit-boundary investigation, 1 October 2026

Five of ten bundled `fan:lldb-240/JEFFPCK6.DAT` levels remain unverified. On `Training 01 - Let's Climb!` (#0), assigning all ten Climbers makes all ten walk across the exit at foot y=128, exactly the current trigger's excluded bottom edge (`x=912…915`, `y=120…127`); the exact bundled run saves **0/10**. A scratch simulation that changes only this exit trigger's bottom edge from 128 to 129 saves **10/10** by tick **1,304**. That altered simulation is **not** a valid bundled-level win and supplies no ledger score or replay digest.

On `Training 04 - Let's Block!` (#3), a four-Blocker route at ticks 140, 420, 700 and 910 sends the remaining six lemmings across the exit platform at foot y=136, again exactly the excluded bottom edge (`x=784…787`, `y=128…135`). It saves **0/10** under the exact bundled rules. The same commands with only that exit trigger's bottom edge extended from 136 to 137 save **6/10**, meeting the rescue requirement, by tick **1,310**. A separate 723-case single-Blocker sweep found no win. `Training 09 - Let's Go!` (#8) also has solid ground at its trigger's current excluded bottom edge, y=132, but no route to that exit has yet been tested. This is an **exit-coordinate parity hypothesis**, not proof that the source game includes the bottom edge: the fan pack may itself contain misplaced exits. No production physics change or score has been made.

On `Training 08 - Let's Bash!` (#7), 312 single-Basher assignments to the first two lemmings produced no save; 253 assignments were accepted and the deepest reached foot y=51. With a first Basher fixed at tick 75, a further 101 second-Basher timings on lemming 0 produced no save; 97 were accepted. Some second assignments open a fall to y=164 and lose the lemmings. These are bounded search results, not an impossibility finding. Scratch controllers, traces, altered-trigger probes and terrain scans are retained in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/jeff-training`. The ledger remains **2,148** verified Classic fan wins, **322** NeoLemmix wins and **3,875** bundled non-official levels without a strict winning replay and replay-based score. Independent source physics parity is still unverified.

The standard Golems-clock beam solver also searched `Training 05 - Let's Build!` (#4) at width 256 with 40-tick fallback/refire intervals and progress-first ranking, exhausting 35,084 states in 11 seconds with **0/5** saved. The same settings on `Training 08 - Let's Bash!` (#7) exhausted its 60-second bound after 174,176 states with **0/10** saved. Neither search proves impossibility or changes the ledger.

## Lemmini source-family classification, 1 October 2026

The current LLDB pages for bundled packs **200, 201, 203, 204, 206, 208, 209, 211, 215, 369, 396, 397, 401, 402, 406, 408, 415, 416, 466 and 469** label their downloads as Lemmini level packs. Examples are [pack 200](https://lldb.camanis.net/levelpack/200), [pack 397](https://lldb.camanis.net/levelpack/397) and [pack 469](https://lldb.camanis.net/levelpack/469). The label appears in each pack's Levels section; [Classic pack 1](https://lldb.camanis.net/levelpack/1) lists its levels instead. The exact bundled archives contain **299** level rows across those 20 packs: **292** `.ini` and **seven** `.lvl` entries in pack 397. Two levels in pack 416 have a verified native Classic win and replay-based score; the other **297** do not. The ledger records `source_engine=Lemmini` for those rows while retaining `source=Classic fan` as the current native playback lane. Source engines for the other Classic-lane packs remain unclassified in this field. The pack classification is stored in `source-engine-families.json` and applied by the ledger and learning-path generator.

The bundled importer currently converts these `.ini` files into `ClassicLevel`. Classic's playable height ends at y=163. The pinned native hatch and exit obstructions above include Lemmini rows with source coordinates beyond that height. This explains those native failures as an engine-family mismatch; it does not prove that any source Lemmini puzzle is unsolvable. The architecture requires a source-correct parser and engine lane before a Lemmini compatibility claim. A strict native win and difficulty score remain open for **297** Lemmini-pack rows, and independent source-physics comparisons remain open for **all 299**. The other **3,571** unverified Classic-lane fan rows and **three** NeoLemmix Introduction Pack rows also remain open. The total non-official backlog is still **3,871**.


`Above The Pepsi Max.ini` was removed from the Golden Learning Path because its source pack is Lemmini. Its optional builder-to-floater teaching objective is unavailable under the source-family exclusion; other distinct applications keep the path at **292** lessons, **156** official and **136** fan, from **1,548** eligible candidates. The generated report still has zero duplicate objectives, zero preparation gaps and a largest curriculum-demand step of **48/1000**. The generator also retains its single-player filter. All 11 `LearningJourneyTests` passed from the existing test build, and the changed Swift journey tool compiled against the current `NxlvKit` module. SwiftPM's full journey script could not run because the manifest sandbox was denied; its export, selection, build and report stages ran separately with matching Xcode tooling. This checks native selection and generation, not source-engine parity.

## Exact-version Beam Them Over source-state check, 1 October 2026

The 2022 `Beam Them Over` replay SHA-256 `79b380a9441103da3ae96dcb0092b75959e5ad3bf539161779c76ce937775691` targets level ID `x4FBBAA4D5D4097BD`, version zero. The native trace used a byte-identical copy of the bundled level, SHA-256 `8781f3feacb2980e755f03005697ba4c6ee3e56fddd0c7dca3b3b1044c29ec6e`. Under the current paired-check library, the first two recorded assignment states match before input: lemming 0 at `(28,64)`, facing left at frame 109, and lemming 2 at `(295,288)`, facing right at frame 274. The third does not match. The replay records lemming 2 at `(268,31)`, facing right at frame 428; native playback has it at `(296,37)`, facing left. Native lemming 2 enters the lower teleporter at frame 377 and leaves its receiver at frame 412. The separate exact-level paired check still saves **0/20**, rejects eight later assignments and reports twelve state divergences. Its input and outputs are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/beam-current-recheck`; the per-frame trace source is ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/beam-frame-trace.swift`.

The pinned Community Edition checkout's `l2_space` teleporter and receiver `.nxmo` files are byte-identical to their 3 October 2019 `c141f345` versions. They specify 26 animation frames with key frames 11 and 25, respectively. This checks two source assets independently of the native win result. It does not identify why the route diverges between frames 274 and 428, prove that the 2022 replay used the bundled level text or establish full source physics parity. No winning replay, digest or score was assigned.

## Beam Them Over teleporter contention trace, 1 October 2026

A fresh build of the current NeoLemmix simulation traced the exact bundled `Beam Them Over` level and the 2022 replay cited above. The lower pair-4 teleporter has a resolved trigger at `(294,316)` with size `4×5`; its receiver trigger is at `(295,37)`. Native lemming 4 enters that teleporter at frame 341. Lemming 2 lands on its trigger at frame 347 while the teleporter and receiver are active, walks away, and enters on its next pass at frame 377. It resumes walking from the receiver at frame 412. Native lemming 2 reaches the replay's recorded third-assignment position and direction, `(268,31)` right, around frame 459, approximately 31 frames after the replay records them at frame 428.

The pinned Community Edition source's `FindGadgetID` checks receiver activity before accepting a teleporter, so the native contention rule has source support. The trace does not show whether the replay's source run had the same lemming-4 position, level and style data or gadget state at frame 341. No physics change, native win or score follows. The current trace source and output are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/beam-current-recheck/trace-current`.

## Classic solver deadline check, 1 October 2026

The solver checked its time limit after expanding every choice for a decision node. A single node could exceed the configured limit by several minutes. `Tools/ClassicSolver/main.swift` now checks the deadline before each node and choice, and every 64 simulation ticks during an advance. The change retains any full winning route already found and still replays the proposed route to check the outcome before it reports a win.

A rebuilt solver completed a two-second `Everyone But The Bomber` probe in 4.0 seconds including process startup and replay checking. Its internal search reported two seconds. The seven-lemming `se7en` probe completed in 10.9 seconds including process startup at a ten-second limit; its earlier 40-second configuration had reported 258 seconds. As a regression check, `Lemmings mountain aventure` still found its known **1/1** win with exactly **51,175** expanded states and a replay file byte-identical to the earlier solver output (SHA-256 `0aff43b9bd0cc52966efdce7c8c676f0d4094758b45bbad3f0471d6ac5aea108`). Scratch attempts and the rebuilt binary are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/deadline-*` and `ClassicSolverDeadline`. These checks verify the deadline fix on two difficult levels and one existing win. They do not add a new ledger win or establish physics parity.

## Infinity teleporter-spacing check, 1 October 2026

The exact bundled `Infinity` level locks its spawn interval at 23, so release-rate input cannot separate the lemmings at the lower teleporter. A current-library native trace of the 2022 archived replay shows lemming 0 walking to the left teleporter at tick 173, leaving its receiver at tick 212, entering the lower teleporter at tick 254, and leaving that receiver at tick 293. It then falls past the lower trigger while lemming 2 occupies the receiver and is removed at tick 339. At ticks 290–330, the skill stock contains no Floater, Climber or Stacker. Lemming 0 is teleporting or falling during the tested window.

A bounded 42-route native probe inserted one Walker, Basher or Platformer command for lemming 0 or 2 at ticks 270–330 in ten-tick steps, keeping the archived commands. Every inserted assignment was rejected for an invalid current action; none won. The probe source, acceptance results and trace are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/infinity-spacing-search.swift`, `infinity-spacing-search.log` and `infinity-early-trace.log`. The paired archived replay remains **0/3** on the exact bundled level. This check identifies why that insertion window cannot resolve the collision, but does not rule out an earlier route change or establish source parity. `Infinity` retains a blank winning-replay digest and score.
