## Golems effect lookup source check, 1 October 2026

The pinned Golems preview 7 assembly's `GameState.GetEffect` reads a quantised effect-map cell at `x >> 2` and `1 + ((YPlus16 - 16) >> 2)`. Its `LevelRenderer.RenderGadgetEffect` also places gadget effects on quantised cells. The native Classic simulation instead tests the lemming's foot point against a half-open pixel rectangle for each trigger. The source IL bodies and assembly SHA-256 are now pinned in `Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_golems_exit_time.py`; that verifier passes. This identifies a source/native collision-lookup difference that needs an exact state comparison before a Golems physics-parity claim.

If the two games place a JEFFPCK6 lemming at the same foot height, the source lookup does **not** resolve either tested exit edge: foot y=128 on `Training 01` selects effect-map row 33, beyond that exit's rows 30–31; foot y=136 on `Training 04` selects row 35, beyond that exit's rows 32–33. The source run's actual lemming positions have not been measured. This static comparison does **not** prove either level is unwinnable in Golems or justify changing native rules, replay status or scores. The three Introduction Pack replay failures and the **3,871**-level non-official backlog are unchanged.

## Bundled levels that cannot meet their current rescue goals, 2 October 2026

The exact bundled-level [structural register](classic-structural-limits.json) and its verifier still identify **four** Classic fan levels for which a native win cannot exist under the current level data and rules. `fan:lldb-386/Gronklems -1.dat#1` has **20** lemmings but requires **37** rescues; the live [source level page](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/386/2) also states 37 of 20. `fan:lldb-437/LDChallenge08.dat#1`, `fan:lldb-508/KillerMaster 1 Wicked 1.dat#3` (`Type this Word!`) and `fan:lldb-509/KillerMaster 1 Havoc 2.dat#7` (`Choose your lucky PASSWORD!`) each have **zero functional exit triggers** in the exact native level. The [two named source pages](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/508/4/Type-this-Word) [also warn](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/509/18/Choose-your-lucky-PASSWORD) that the levels may be unsolvable; these warnings are context, while the exact native descriptor is the structural proof. The [fourth source page](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/437/2) does not carry that warning. The public pages were checked live on 2 October. A winning replay and replay-based score for **every** currently bundled non-official level therefore requires a content decision for these four: provide corrected level files, remove them from the playable bundle, or explicitly exempt them as invalid. None of these choices is applied in the ledger. The other **3,866** unverified non-official levels remain in scope, and full physics parity remains unverified.

The separate [published `Pinpoint Accuracy` replay](https://lldb.camanis.net/level/430/3) changes the release rate to **99** and assigns the sole Builder to lemming 0 at source cycle **58** (decoded replay SHA-256 `042a6744b089fe315a2d7c4c05e91356a154d6f34b2d5b1fc4f94bd7ce2239ad`). The unchanged replay was run in the independent Golems browser player, which saved **4/10** at cycle **362**. A separate source run with no commands saved **0/10** by cycle **125**; this control does not isolate which of the two simultaneous commands causes the source win. The source `LDChallenge01.dat` bytes match those inside bundled `fan:lldb-430` (DAT SHA-256 `e0eb4fbd6f41836d7d29c9a35a8e274a6c7c6032f78419d137ca7c060dbacf05`; bundled archive SHA-256 `bdbf943f510410b7e4c304ff57a31b83134d51b32a6aeed2432eaa6f2804d835`). The native initial-state hash matches `24dd45af88f254d5e3479f9f055af419ac5a4dccb92088d015f710d6b7468d3e`, with the same **4/10** goal, one stocked Builder and four functional exits. On the current native engine, lemming 0 is still falling at tick **58**, so the published Builder assignment is rejected. All ten lemmings are lost by tick **156**. This is a measured source/native replay outcome mismatch, not a full diagnosis of the physics rule. The [independent check](classic-independent-source-checks.json) records the digests and results.

Sixteen one-command shifts within eight ticks did not win natively. A distinct **2,010**-input sweep assigned the Builder to each of ten lemmings at every tick from 0 through 200; none won. As a timing control, moving lemming 0's Builder to native tick **89** made that assignment valid, but it still saved **0/10**: nine lemmings fell by tick 156, and the Builder died at tick 316. This shows that shifting the command by the observed 31-cycle difference between the two no-command endings is insufficient; it does not identify the underlying rule. The source player results, native traces, candidate files and bounded sweep are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/pinpoint-source-20261002`. These losses do not prove the level impossible or establish full source physics parity. The native replay digest and difficulty score remain blank.

An isolated native-library trial changed only the entrance-open tick from **35** to **4**. Its first hatch occurred at tick **23**, and its no-input run lost all ten lemmings by tick **125**, numerically matching the source no-input completion cycle. The unchanged published Builder assignment at tick **58** was then accepted, but the scratch run still lost **10/10** by tick **285**. Enabling all Golems object slots in a second scratch run gave the same loss. Subsequent independent source frames showed no visible lemming after **54** `Tick()` calls and one after **58**. The scratch 23-tick hatch therefore does **not** reproduce the source's visible release timing; its matching no-input end cycle was misleading. The source's exact hatch and replay-command application cycles remain unmeasured. The scratch traces and source frame captures are under `pinpoint-source-20261002`; this isolated trial changes neither the shipped engine nor the ledger's replay and score status.

The source player also requested `GROUND4O.DAT` and `VGAGR4.DAT` for this Crystal level. Their downloaded bytes match the native files in `Sources/Ports/lemmings_dos_1991-07-30` exactly: SHA-256 `9669ee978e5adf5f8d5bfeb80ccc5d6c20e75e32f55e2a5c1c738f64af8faa91` and `40a99d3d9540609da9d6a74c07a832786818aed361326e37c01a909a1615921d`. A graphics-file byte mismatch is therefore ruled out; parsing and rendering parity remain untested. The source `GOLEMS.DAT` was also captured in the ignored scratch directory, but its engine and physics data are not native parity evidence.

The source canvas is displayed at three times its 455×200 logical size and begins 41.5 CSS pixels below the page top. Correcting for that offset makes the visible upper and lower terrain bands consistent with the native solid-mask rows near y=84 and y=102. This rules out the apparent vertical offset inferred from uncorrected screenshots; exact pixel-by-pixel terrain parity remains untested. The remaining early divergence is the lemming's route after falling from the hatch.

The source replay and a no-input control were captured at the same `Tick()` counts. By tick **60**, the published replay visibly changes lemming 0's state while the control still shows a faller. An isolated native build kept the shipped hatch timing and allowed the replay Builder assignment while lemming 0 was falling. Its first diagnostic replay retained the Builder command but omitted the source's simultaneous release-rate command. That replay passed strict native replay verification on the exact bundled level: **4/10** saved at tick **383**, with replay file SHA-256 `61f4ab4c46b1ed9ff8ec76c62bf8c5b48cdd03d776f0caaf02b8d2b67dbe48c4`. Restoring the release-rate 99 command in a second scratch playback still saved **4/10** at tick **383**. The scratch library SHA-256 is `cbdd22243e9631dac6532c1c48256bbd2fdf8ae37e4effc8937b8ede246ac9d7`. The source player saves **4/10** at cycle **362** with both commands. This scratch win remains a diagnostic result: the source replay path bypasses the interactive falling guard, but the 21-cycle difference and untested intermediate states prevent a physics-parity claim. The scratch source, replay, source frame captures and strict verifier log are under `pinpoint-source-20261002/airbuilder` and `pinpoint-source-20261002`.

The same isolated rule was applied to **16** recorded unscored published routes on their exact bundled archives. It produced **zero** further wins. One route, `fan:lldb-225/ANTHPCK5.DAT#3`, advanced past its earlier Builder rejection at tick **462** and then stopped at a Basher rejection at tick **521**. The other routes either retained their input rejection or still lost. This bounded batch helps locate replay-state differences; it does not justify changing the shipped engine or assigning those levels scores. Its results are in `pinpoint-source-20261002/airbuilder/recent-results.log`.

A second scratch build also allowed a falling Basher assignment. It advanced that one route past tick **521**, but the next Basher for lemming 4 was rejected at tick **809**. All **16** exact-level routes still had **zero** wins. The source interactive Basher predicate rejects falling, while its replay action path calls the assignment method directly. This trial does not establish replay or physics parity. Its results are in `pinpoint-source-20261002/airbuilderbasher/recent-results.log`.

## Pinpoint Accuracy source assignment rule, 2 October 2026

The pinned Golems Core assembly SHA-256 `38cb69f18f5055821a496336a948fc30596e56c607c3ddddfb4675be78a5873e` was inspected independently of native playback. Its `GolemActivity.Falling` enum value is **3**. For that activity, `Golem.set_Activity` stores flag `1 << (3 - 1)`, or **4**. The interactive Builder and Basher selectors reject activities that intersect masks `0x7aef` and `0x7b6f` respectively; both masks include flag 4. In contrast, `ReplayableGameState.AdvanceCore` calls `GameState.TryAdvance`, which calls `TryPerformAction`. That method calls `AssignBuildSkill` directly, without `GetAssignBuildSkillGolemIndex` or its falling predicate. Thus the interactive guard cannot establish replay assignment eligibility. The pinned method bodies and assembly digest are checked by `Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/verify_golems_exit_time.py`. The metadata inspection is in ignored `pinpoint-source-20261002/inspect_golems_methods.py`.

The scratch win is consistent with a source replay path that assigns Builder while lemming 0 is falling, but it does not prove the source lemming state at that cycle. The original ten-byte source replay also has a **release-rate 99** command in its only event record; an earlier diagnostic candidate omitted it. Re-decoding with `Tools/DifficultyDiagnostics/decode_golems_replays.py` recovered both commands. On the exact bundled level, a current native replay containing only that rate command saved **0/10** by tick **156** and reproduced the audited initial-state hash. A current native replay containing both source commands still rejected the Builder at tick **58**. The rate-only source fragment could not be loaded by the independent browser player (`GenericInvalidData`), so the source contribution of each command remains unmeasured. Exact source hatch and command cycles remain unmeasured. The current source/native replay result is an outcome mismatch with a candidate replay eligibility cause, not a complete physics diagnosis. No shipped engine rule, ledger win, difficulty score or physics-parity claim changed; **3,870** bundled non-official levels still lack a verified native win.

## Classic solver no-win diagnosis, 2 October 2026

Repeated zero-save search batches prompted a solver calibration against eight exact-archive fan levels that already have strict native wins. Seven known wins use one skill input; the eighth changes only the release rate. The earlier width-256, 15-second beam search recovered four of eight. A current-source rebuild still missed `Helled cavern!` at width 256. Its one-Basher replay wins **50/15** at tick **1,746** under both the earlier linked library and the current library, with the same initial-state hash and strict replay outcome. The older library linkage is a batch reproducibility gap, but does not explain this control failure.

The search trace for `Helled cavern!` reaches tick **150** and offers lemming 0, but at width 1 it retains a competing tick-149 Basher route after the known tick-150 input. Forcing the known input and allowing only waiting wins; ordinary beam ranking drops that route. `Jingle Lemming` has a second coverage gap: the normal 25-tick fallback skips its known tick-132 Basher opportunity. A one-tick fallback exposes it.

`ClassicSolver --rollout-single` now checks the no-more-input continuation of each distinct one-skill candidate before beam pruning. With width 1, a one-tick fallback and a 15-second bound, it recovered **7/7** known one-skill wins. The rate-only control remains outside this mode. All seven generated replays passed a separate strict current-library playback on their exact bundled ZIPs; archive and replay SHA-256 values are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/solver-calibration-20261002/rollout-strict-checks.json`. These are already-scored controls, so the ledger gains no new level or score. A failed search still does not prove a level impossible, and this calibration does not establish source-physics parity.

The same source-built solver then checked the **eight** unscored low-rescue levels whose preceding wide-beam batch had completed before it was interrupted. This focused width-1, 15-second, one-tick-fallback pass used the exact archived ZIPs and found **zero** one-skill wins. Its attempts and archive checks are in ignored `.build/full-difficulty-evaluation/clock-rescore/solver-calibration-20261002/unscored-sample`. This result limits the fix's immediate yield on that sample; it does not clear multi-input routes, level-data defects or source-physics differences. The non-official backlog remains **3,870**.

Search coverage explains why these eight failures need caution. A traced repeat of `What's Better Than Bubbles?` used its 15-second limit by waiting-route tick **83** with one-tick fallback. A separate 25-tick fallback reached waiting-route tick **193** and evaluated **73** distinct one-skill continuations in the same time, without a win. The current solver reports waiting-route tick and continuation count for this mode. A 15-second failure does not cover the remaining level timeline or all skill timings. The source site was unavailable during this check, so no published solution or source-engine outcome was established for this level.

## Single-skill timeline sweep and Diving Area trace, 2 October 2026

`ClassicSolver --sweep-single` now checks each active lemming and available skill on every tick of a fixed-rate, no-input route. They do not clear other release rates, multi-skill routes or source-physics differences. No new replay or difficulty score was added; the current non-official backlog is **3,736**.

A separate sweep of `fan:lldb-549/Ji Hoon Earth 1.DAT#1` at release rates **25, 50, 75 and 99** completed every one-skill continuation on each viable no-input route, with **zero** wins. The four rates tried **38,414** assignments in total; the exact results are in ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/sweep-rate-549/results.json`. This narrows that level's search gap to multi-input routes or a source/runtime difference without proving either. A rate-99 control on the already-scored `fan:lldb-3/Anatol00.dat#3` still won under strict native replay.

The sweep now retains a better partial saved count when it finds one. A rebuild with that diagnostic change repeated the three complete timeline searches above and still saved **0** on each; `Jingle Lemming` still won **50/25**. The repeat logs and partial outputs are under ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/sweep-partial-recheck`.

For `Diving Area`, the exact bundled level and graphics bytes match the independent Golems source trial. A current native trace of the published route places lemming 2 at foot **(709, 100)** on tick **310**, then at **(709, 135)** when it starts splatting on tick **322**; it is lost on tick **338**. The native fall exceeded its 60-pixel safe threshold. This explains the native loss under the current rule, but the source worker's corresponding position has not been measured directly. Source screenshots cannot identify that worker with enough certainty to isolate a physics difference. No speculative physics rule changed, and source parity remains open. The native trace is under ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/diving-trace.log`.

A terrain comparison localised an earlier route difference. The source screenshot at cycle **250** and a native solid-mask snapshot at tick **250** align best at a horizontal viewport offset of **569** pixels, with **7,443/7,600** sampled terrain classifications agreeing. At level row **36**, the source shows an opening at x **678–685** while the native opening is at x **652–660**. The source opening was absent at cycle **145**. The pixel classification, source screenshot digest and native-mask digest are recorded in ignored `.build/golems-next-promotion/diving-mask-alignment.json`. Native lemming 0 starts digging at x **656** on tick **197**; moving that command 26 ticks later moves its digger to x **682**, but exact native replay still saves only **3/4**, with lemming 2 splatting on tick **338**. The shifted opening alone therefore does not repair the outcome. The source Basher movement and subsequent worker states need direct parity checks before an engine rule can change.

An independent headless source-player recheck again saved **4/4** at cycle **1,417**. New screenshots localise lemming 0's earlier divergence. At cycle **130**, the source sprite is around x **613–615** and the native worker is bashing at foot x **608**. At cycle **145**, the source sprite has advanced to x **628–631** while the native worker is still bashing at x **613**. By cycle **195**, before the Digger input, the source sprite is at x **678–681** and the native walker is at x **654**. Sprite pixels only approximate the source foot position, and the source action enum was not inspected. The movement points to Basher termination or terrain removal before the Digger, not a justified global speed change. [The divergence evidence](golems-diving-basher-divergence.json) records screenshot and native-trace digests, the pixel extraction method, and each sampled position. No new win or score follows from this comparison.

A fresh current-source native build produced the same initial-state hash and **2/4** outcome. At cycle **130**, source screenshot pixels at level x **617–618**, rows **28, 30, 32 and 34**, are black while the native solid mask retains both pixels on every sampled row. This localises a terrain-removal or Basher-position difference before the Digger. A scratch-only Golems Bash-mask shift of one to four pixels did not reproduce the source win: shifting every Basher saved at most **3/4**, and shifting lemming 0 alone also saved at most **3/4**. With the latter three-pixel shift, moving the Digger across ticks **190–202** still saved **3/4** each time; the changed early tunnel let lemming 2 reach the fall sooner. The [mask probe](golems-diving-bash-mask-probe.json) records exact source and native terrain digests, outcomes and tested offsets. This rejects a simple global mask displacement; no production physics rule or ledger score changed.

## Diving Area shaft route split, 3 October 2026

A focused headless and muted source run captured the digging shaft at cycles **900–930**. The native worker 2 walks left to **(678, 126)** at tick **911**, turns right at **(677, 126)** on tick **912**, and falls on the right side at tick **918**. A source worker at the same shaft is visibly falling left by cycle **915**. At cycle **930**, the source has workers near world X **663** and **691**; the native workers are at X **683** and **692**. By cycle **960**, one source worker trails its native counterpart by roughly **43** pixels while the other three routes stay close. The [shaft comparison](classic-golems-diving-shaft-parity.json) pins both strict outcomes, source screenshots, native trace, replay and archive digests.

Native row **126** at X **678–686** remains solid until tick **918**. Source screenshots show changing pixels around that row by cycle **912**, but the sprites overlap the terrain, so these pixels do not prove the exact source removal tick. The earlier left-facing Basher tunnel now explains the route split more directly than a Digger timing hypothesis: source and native initial terrain match at all **16,281** sampled pixels. Between source cycles **678** and **685**, row **126** becomes black at X **704–708**, while the native mask retains those five pixels through tick **685** and begins clearing on row **127**. By cycle **900** the source tunnel is one level pixel higher. In the measured X **645–725**, Y **118–140** region, **53** row-126 pixels are clear only in the source image and **56** row-135 pixels are clear only in native terrain.

A scratch engine change moved only Golems left-facing Basher terrain removal up one pixel. It reduced the region's screenshot/terrain difference from **183** to **76** pixels and gave an exact bundled-level strict **4/4** win at tick **1,414**, close to the source player’s cycle **1,417**; the production engine already wins **4/4** at tick **1,373**. The scratch change broke **62 of 152** existing strict Golems winning replays: **34** state-hash mismatches, **17** completion-tick mismatches, **7** rejected commands and **4** saved-count mismatches. The pinned preview DLL's assignment IL gives both Basher directions the same Y offset, but binary identity with the browser's Golems.Core.wasm has not been established. The precise live-source mask decoding or sprite-foot rule still needs confirmation. The scratch change was **not** promoted. [The shaft comparison](classic-golems-diving-shaft-parity.json) now records screenshot, trace, scratch-library and regression-audit digests. This check adds no new ledger win or score and does not establish full physics parity.

## A TOWERING PROBLEM Builder retimes, 3 October 2026

The optimised solver attempted **14** forced first-Builder timings from tick **58** through **72** (excluding tick **65**) on the exact bundled `t3tesla.dat#8` level at release rate **90**. Each run allowed one additional skill continuation with width **64** and five-tick fallback/refire intervals. All retained **8/9** at best and none won. The runs made **113,353** expansions and **1,289** completed one-skill continuations. A later input audit found that the selected partials for ticks **58–60** omitted their forced Builder, so those three runs do not test the stated retimings. The other **11** selected partials contain their forced Builder. [The retime record](classic-solver-tower-retimes.json) pins the original results; [the corrected check](classic-tower-invalid-prefix-recheck.json) records this limit. No score or parity result changed.

## Two-skill control and small-population no-win probes, 3 October 2026

The optimised solver recovered an already-scored `Under The Bridge` route with two Miner assignments in **46,407** expansions. A separate current-library verifier replayed it on its exact bundled archive and confirmed **78/40** saved at tick **2,427**. This establishes that the present beam search can recover at least this two-skill route, but does not establish general multi-skill coverage or source parity.

Two unscored, previously untouched small-population levels received targeted checks. The complete default-rate one-skill sweeps on `A toe Take 2` and `Get Out of the Pool!` each saved **0**; their viable waiting timelines ended at ticks **101** and **1,022**, with **94** and **9,348** completed continuations. Focused multi-skill searches with width **64** also saved **0/10** and **0/17**, respectively. [The calibration record](classic-solver-two-skill-calibration.json) pins the exact archives, initial states, native binaries, strict control replay and bounded settings. Neither negative search proves impossibility or source-physics mismatch. No new win or score was added.

## Two-skill beam coverage diagnosis, 3 October 2026

The optimised width-64 beam missed **three** already-scored two-skill controls: `Blow two lemmings` (**0/2**), `Lack of builders` (**0/80**) and `Rent-a-Lemming` (**0/25**) in 12-second, five-tick-fallback runs. A one-tick fallback also missed `Blow two lemmings` after **46,759** expansions. When supplied only the first known command, the solver found a second command for both `Blow two lemmings` and `Rent-a-Lemming`. Separate strict playback on each exact bundled archive confirmed **2/2** and **50/25** wins. These are already-scored levels, so no ledger score changed. [The coverage record](classic-solver-multiskill-coverage.json) pins archives, states, binaries, commands and replay digests. The native engine can win these controls; ordinary beam failures are search-coverage failures. This does not diagnose each unscored level or establish source parity.

A scratch policy offered three active walkers at each fallback decision. It failed to recover the already-scored `Under The Bridge` control at width **64**, although the default policy recovered it, and added no win on the two small-population no-win levels. The policy was reverted. The next solver change should retain viable first-skill routes through later decision points without crowding out the calibrated controls.

## Balanced solver ranking and resume check, 3 October 2026

The solver now has an optional `--balanced-ranking` mode that reserves half its beam for survivor-first routes and half for progress-first routes. With width **128**, it recovered both calibrated two-skill controls that had required different rankings: `Under The Bridge` (**78/40**) and `Blow two lemmings` (**2/2**). Separate strict playback confirmed both generated replays on their exact bundled archives. The unchanged default one-skill control regenerated the same replay SHA-256. These are already-scored controls; the change does not add a ledger win.

The batch driver records the balanced mode and now checks every completed prior configuration for an identity before repeating a search. A synthetic default/balanced/default/balanced sequence made only its first two attempts. A targeted balanced search on unscored `The Turnaround!` reached **99/100**. One further skill after its full eight-command partial had no active continuation; replacing its last Blocker with one skill tested **57** continuations without winning. A balanced continuation after its first seven commands also remained at **99/100**. [The balanced ranking record](classic-solver-balanced-ranking.json) pins binary and replay digests, settings and bounded results. No new difficulty score or independent source-physics result was added.

## The Turnaround! near-win diagnosis, 3 October 2026

The exact bundled level requires **100/100**. The new **99/100** partial leaves lemming 1 blocking at x=992, foot y=52 until completion tick **1,022**; all other lemmings exit. The route uses the level's complete skill supply. Deleting the Blocker causes this route to save **0/100**, while five earlier single-command deletions still save **99/100** and free a Basher, Digger or Miner. This identifies the remaining task: preserve the turnaround and release or replace the Blocker.

A current-library probe completed **259/329** single retimes or deletions, **901/1,344** paired retimes without the Blocker, and **2,025/2,025** one-skill substitutions around the Blocker using one freed skill. None won; the best remained **99/100**. Two current-source solver continuations with a freed Basher or Digger completed **815** and **810** one-skill rollouts through waiting tick **1,022**, also without a win. [The blocker investigation](classic-turnaround-blocker-investigation.json) pins the archive, initial state, library, probe and partial-replay digests. The searches are bounded, so this is a route diagnosis rather than an impossibility or source-physics claim. The ledger has no new score.

## Passive-route search baseline, 3 October 2026

The exact bundled `Go Fetch!` saves **22/23** without input by tick **1,392**. A 30-second balanced beam stopped with a **6/23** partial because its waiting branch had not reached that outcome. The solver now completes the fixed-rate, no-input route before beam expansion when there is no forced prefix. A two-second run retained the **22/23** passive partial. The already-scored `Only Float is Survive` control regenerated its byte-identical release-rate-50 replay and passed separate strict **1/1** playback. [The passive baseline record](classic-solver-passive-baseline.json) pins both solver results, replay and archive digests. This corrects the search lead; it does not add a win, score or independent source-physics result.

A subsequent focused `Go Fetch!` run preserved the **22/23** baseline and searched **1,396** one-skill continuations with a 20-tick decision interval. Its waiting route reached tick **1,392**; no continuation won. The direct solver now creates missing output folders before writing replay or partial files, after an earlier otherwise-complete search failed at that write. The same strict **1/1** control regenerated its byte-identical replay in a new nested folder. [The rollout record](classic-solver-fetch-rollout.json) pins the current solver, exact archive, partial and control digests. A multi-skill route and independent source physics comparison remain open.

# Extended canvas geometry probe (3 October 2026)

The exact bundled `Gronklems #4` archive, entry `Good Reception.ini`, has source objects and terrain outside the Classic 1600×160 canvas. A current-source native render retained **7,179** solid pixels at DOS size. The new explicit 2200×400 render retained **52,288**, including **45,109** beyond DOS bounds. An invalid 4097-pixel-wide canvas was rejected. The renderer default remains DOS size, and the direct solver now accepts explicit canvas dimensions for focused geometry probes. This addresses one concrete clipping defect exposed by the no-win data; it does not select Lemmini physics or make the app use the larger canvas.

The source-built solver searched this exact archive at width 32 under a 10-second bound. Its default canvas expanded 6 states and the experimental canvas expanded 10; both saved 0/74. No winning replay or difficulty score was added. The archive, library and solver SHA-256 values, canvas counts and search results are in `classic-extended-canvas-probe.json`. The optimised `NxlvKit` and solver builds succeeded, and a direct native geometry probe checked both canvases and the invalid-size guard. Independent source-physics parity remains open.

A read-only scan of the 20 identified Lemmini-source pack ZIPs found 272 `.ini` members with parsed terrain or object placements. All 272 have a terrain origin at y≥160; 264 also have one at x≥1600. This identifies widespread Classic clipping in that source-family subset. It does not count unique ledger identities or prove these levels can be won under Classic physics. Two focused 3200×400, one-skill searches on two-lemming `grams88` levels found no win: `Calder Glen` reached waiting tick 147 with one completed rollout, and `08 BC lost temple` reached tick 169 with two. Separate native traces found first losses at ticks 147 and 116. The extended canvas alone is therefore insufficient for these examples. As a default-canvas regression control, the current-source solver regenerated the existing `Only Float is Survive` replay byte-for-byte; separate strict playback won **1/1** at tick **375**. The same probe record pins these results. No ledger score or source-physics parity result changed.

The `.ini` importer revealed a second geometry defect: it routed text terrain through the DOS record's signed 9-bit Y field. In `Good Reception`, source y=296 became native y=-216. Of the 272 parsed `.ini` members above, 265 contain a terrain y beyond that DOS positive range. An explicit source-coordinate import now preserves text terrain positions for focused probes; the default app and ledger import remain unchanged. At 2200×400, this increased `Good Reception`'s retained solid pixels from **52,288** to **58,917**. A 3200×400 source-coordinate one-skill search on `08 BC lost temple` advanced the waiting route from tick **169** with **2** rollouts to tick **232** with **40**; it still found no win. `Calder Glen` remained at zero. The current-source default solver regenerated the same byte-identical `Only Float is Survive` replay, and a rebuilt strict validator won **1/1** at tick **375**. The new parser test covers the y=296 round trip; `swift test --disable-sandbox --filter FanTextTerrainTests` passed **1/1** after the ordinary SwiftPM manifest sandbox rejected the initial run. Direct source builds and native probes also passed. These experimental results do not establish Lemmini physics parity or justify a difficulty score.

## Binary records inside Lemmini-tagged packs (3 October 2026)

The pack-level source label concealed a useful format split. The exact bundled corpus has **299** levels in the 20 packs tagged Lemmini: **272** text `.ini` levels, **20** 2048-byte binary Classic records named `.ini` in `fan:lldb-416`, and **seven** binary `.lvl` records in `fan:lldb-397`. All 27 binary members and both ZIP SHA-256 values were checked. The library already routes those members through its Classic binary parser. Two binary levels have existing strict native wins; **25** remain unverified. This corrects the earlier shorthand that all 297 unverified rows need a text source parser. The source-engine label and parity status stay unchanged: binary encoding alone does not establish source physics.

The two existing binary-member ledger replays passed rebuilt strict playback on the exact bundled `fan:lldb-416` archive: `Above The Pepsi Max` saved **2/2** at tick **2,258**, and `Puzzle Time` saved **50/46** at tick **1,517**. The previously unattempted binary `2010 CONTEST/11Ellischant.lvl` (10 lemmings, nine required) received a focused current-source balanced search: width 128, 45-second bound, 20-tick fallback and refire intervals. It expanded **111,976** states and saved **0/9**. [The binary-member audit](classic-binary-source-pack-audit.json) records the exact archives, replay identities and attempt. The full corpus report now checks the 272/27 format split and retains **2,309** Classic fan wins, **322** NeoLemmix wins and **3,714** non-official levels without a verified replay and score. The learning report passed with post export disabled: **292** lessons, **156** official and **136** fan, zero duplicate objectives or preparation gaps, and a largest demand step of **48/1000**. No new difficulty score or independent source-physics parity result was added.

## Text object-coordinate loss (3 October 2026)

The text `.ini` importer also routes object positions through DOS alignment. Of the **272** text members with objects in Lemmini-tagged packs, **214** have at least one X coordinate changed by that alignment. `Good Reception` object 0 moves from source x=**1,750** to native x=**1,744**. An opt-in source-object-coordinate mode now retains the text positions alongside the source-terrain and extended-canvas probes. The default app and scored ledger import stay unchanged. The parser test passed with the source object and terrain coordinates, and the two already-scored binary `.ini` witnesses still passed current-source strict playback on the exact archive. On `08 BC lost temple`, combining both source-coordinate flags retained the previous **0/1** result after waiting tick **232** and **40** one-skill continuations. [The geometry probe](classic-extended-canvas-probe.json) pins the builds and measurements. More source rules and multi-skill search are required; this adds no score or parity claim.

## Lemmini source-family engine gap, 3 October 2026

A scan of the **272** exact bundled text `.ini` levels found **267** without explicit dimensions. The [related SuperLemminiToo source](https://github.com/Blazingstix/SuperLemminiToo/blob/main/src/lemmini/game/Level.java) defaults those fields to **3200×320**, reads optional dimensions and fall-distance rules, and passes every entrance to its trap-door scheduler. The native Classic renderer defaults to **1600×160**; its simulation retains only four entrances. **Twelve** bundled text members specify five to fourteen entrances, and five specify a custom fall distance. The fork is a source-family reference, not proof of the exact runtime for every pack. [The source-family audit](classic-lemmini-engine-gap.json) records each affected member and all 20 bundled archive digests.

The earlier 3200×400 probes tested larger clipping bounds but did not reproduce the source-family default height, graphics scale, release timing or all entrance rules. Their zero-win outcomes cannot be used to judge level solvability. The native Classic engine needs a separate, source-checked Lemmini playback mode before these text levels can provide reliable parity or difficulty evidence. The scored binary Classic members remain on their existing exact-level path.

The direct solver now has an explicit `--source-canvas` probe. It reads a text level's `width` and `height`, using **3200×320** when absent, and rejects binary records. This keeps source-family canvas experiments reproducible without changing the app or any scored replay. The parser test passed **1/1**, checking both the default and an explicit **3168×320** canvas. The optimised source-built solver compiled. On the exact `08 BC lost temple` member, the source-canvas one-skill probe reached waiting tick **205**, made **50** continuations and saved **0/1**. A binary `.ini` source-canvas request was rejected as intended. [The source-family audit](classic-lemmini-engine-gap.json) pins the rebuilt binaries, archive and probe settings. This bounded result adds no replay, score or parity claim.

The related [SuperLemminiToo game controller](https://github.com/Blazingstix/SuperLemminiToo/blob/main/src/lemmini/game/GameController.java) exposes a further clock and hatch gap. Its logic runs at **30 ms per frame**, opens entrances after **72** frames, starts release after a further **30**, and uses **107 − release rate** frames between lemmings. The first release therefore occurs on the next update after the opening delay, around frame **103**. The native Classic simulation instead uses **17 ticks per second**, opens at tick **35**, starts with a **20-tick** release countdown and uses the DOS release interval. The same source controller cycles all entrances, with a special **0, 1, 2, 1** pattern for three, and its [level loader](https://github.com/Blazingstix/SuperLemminiToo/blob/main/src/lemmini/game/Level.java) positions each hatch from its sprite mask and permits a left-facing entrance. Native Classic uses a fixed DOS hatch offset, releases right-facing lemmings and retains only four entrances. None of the **272** bundled source-tagged text levels declares a custom `entranceOrder`, so this source family's default cycle is the relevant reference if that engine is confirmed for those packs. These are source-family differences, not proven parity results for any bundled level. They explain why changing the canvas alone cannot make the zero-win probes conclusive. A separate Lemmini simulation and source playback comparison remain prerequisites for scoring those levels.

A focused check of the binary `fan:lldb-416/I Love Bubbles.ini#-1` level completed all **40** viable default-rate, single-skill continuations through waiting tick **90**; none saved its only lemming. A calibrated width-96, 35-second two-or-more-skill search expanded **106,689** nodes and also saved **0/1**. These bounds add no replay, score or impossibility claim.

`Which Way Should we Go` is another unscored binary member in the same exact archive. Its source-built one-skill sweep reached waiting tick **5,102**, completed **2,178** continuations and saved **0/1**. A targeted progress-first, width-128 multi-skill search expanded **94,356** states in 30 seconds and also saved **0/1**. The level has three lemmings, needs one rescue and has eleven Builders; its exit is above its entrance. To check whether the solver's sum of all three lemmings' distances hid a useful worker, an opt-in `--rescue-quota-distance` ranking now scores only the nearest lemmings still needed for the rescue target. The changed solver compiled and won the existing `Only Float is Survive` 1/1 control in six expansions. The same bounded search on `Which Way Should we Go` expanded **92,703** states and still saved **0/1**. A second targeted check on the known `Go Fetch!` near-win retained **22/23** after **76,179** expansions, without winning. [The probe record](classic-solver-rescue-quota-probe.json) pins policies, binaries and outcomes. The new ranking is optional; these failures neither prove impossibility nor establish source-family parity, and no ledger score changed.

## Two-skill timing sweep, 4 October 2026

The source-built solver now has an opt-in `--sweep-pair` mode that tries every viable first assignment in a bounded tick window, every viable second assignment in a bounded gap window, and then completes each candidate without further input. It found unforced strict native wins on two already-scored controls that the ordinary beam had missed: `Blow two lemmings` saved **2/2** after **411** completed continuations, and `Rent-a-Lemming` saved **50/25** after **99**. Their replay SHA-256 values are `1feb25520eaf892c6c17c2a4e313b59ab66c4d63441aac68acd06da7d4878030` and `a55dbb665d544a6ccbedd759c352f5b224e1185d0ddc6ad6f8a2d0156bd957d0`. Both proposals passed the solver's strict exact-level native replay before export. A focused unscored `Walking on chains` Builder/Digger sweep completed **13,546** continuations through first tick **150** and saved **0/10**. [The pair-sweep record](classic-solver-pair-sweep.json) pins archives, initial states, binaries, settings and coverage. The control wins establish a concrete search gap; the negative probe does not prove impossibility or source-physics mismatch.

## Crowd skill broadcast recovery, 4 October 2026

The one-skill policy cannot cover a route that needs the same stocked skill on every lemming. A new opt-in `--broadcast-skill` solver policy applies that skill after each hatch and sweeps a bounded hatch-to-assignment delay. `Pillars and builders` has four entrances, four lemmings to save and four Builders. A one-worker-focused search cannot assign Builders to the other three workers. The current direct-distance search under that focus made **47** expansions without a win; a one-tick single-skill pass reached waiting tick **111** with **10** continuations. Removing the focus limit made **671** expansions, also without a win. A separate four-worker Builder broadcast completed **101** hatch-to-assignment delays and made **108** successful assignments without a win. Its cached 29 September source page has no published record. [The updated small-puzzle record](classic-small-builder-search-bound.json) pins the exact archive, page, solver and partial replay digests. The focused search was insufficient for this level, while the unrestricted and broadcast bounds remain incomplete. No physics change, score or parity claim follows from these results.

## Forced-prefix solver correction, 4 October 2026

The `Pillars and builders` no-win data exposed two solver problems. The solver selected a no-input state before the first hatch as its best partial. It also silently skipped a forced Builder at tick **78** when an earlier search input made that assignment invalid. That search's partial replay started with a Builder at tick **76**, so it did not test the requested prefix.

`ClassicSolver` now prefers viable partial routes with skill inputs and released workers when saved counts match. It rejects a branch if a forced skill fails, and it reports a win or partial only when every forced input appears in the replay at the requested tick. The same focused run now retains a legal tick-**78** Builder partial. The corrected forced-prefix continuation made **947** expansions and found no win. A known one-Floater control still wins **1/1** with its prior replay digest. [The calibration record](classic-prefix-enforcement-calibration.json) pins the archive, binaries, before-and-after partials and control replay. No level score or source parity result changed.

A valid forced Floater at tick **54** still produced the same strict **1/1** control replay digest. Adding a later rate command at tick **1,000**, after that route completes, reported `UNSOLVED` and emitted no replay. This checks both a valid prefix and a prefix that the route cannot complete.

## Go Fetch! forced-prefix recheck, 4 October 2026

The earlier `Go Fetch!` forced-prefix search retained a partial replay with **zero inputs**. It did not test the six requested commands. The corrected source-built solver replayed the same exact bundled archive and prefix. Its beam search retained all six forced inputs, expanded **57,909** states and saved at most **22/23**. A separate one-skill rollout also retained all six inputs, reached waiting tick **2,042** and completed **27** continuations after **40,094** expansions. It saved at most **22/23**. [The recheck record](classic-go-fetch-prefix-recheck.json) pins the old and new replay digests, archive, prefix and source-built binaries. These bounds do not cover other timing or multi-skill routes. No new win, score or source parity result was added.

## Tower forced-Builder input audit, 4 October 2026

The historical `A TOWERING PROBLEM` retime batch included three partial replays that omitted their requested Builders at ticks **58–60**. A native trace on the exact bundled level shows lemming 0 falling at those ticks. Its first legal Builder assignment is tick **61**. The corrected solver rejects each of the three early prefixes before a one-skill continuation. It found no win. The other **11** historical retime partials contain their requested Builder. The cached source page lists no winning record, so this native assignment check does not establish source physics parity. [The audit record](classic-tower-invalid-prefix-recheck.json) pins all fourteen old prefix and replay digests, the current archive and solver, and the native trace.
